**       yatra caivātmanātmānaṁ, paśyann ātmani tuṣyati.**

Where (*yatra*) the *citta* comes to complete rest/ come to a standstill (*uparamate*), thoroughly stilled / cessation of mental activities (*niruddham*) through the practice (*sevayā*) of *yoga*; and where (*yatra ca*), Indeed/ precisely (*eva*) seeing/ observing the *ātmā* by the *ātmā*, one is contented / rejoices (*tuṣyati*) in the *ātmā*.

**[21](javascript:void(0))   sukham ātyantikaṁ yat tad, buddhi-grāhyam atīndriyam;**

**       vetti yatra na caivāyaṁ, sthitaś calati tattvataḥ.**

That (*yat tat*) ultimate (*ātyantikam*) bliss (*sukham*), which is to be grasped (*grāhyam*) by awakening/ intellect (*buddhi*) because it Beyond the sensory faculties (*atīndriyam*); where (*yatra*), once firmly established / abiding (*sthitaḥ*), this \[practitioner\] (*ayam*) no longer strays (*na ca eva calati*) from thatness / ultimate reality / truth (*tattvataḥ*).

**[22](javascript:void(0))   yaṁ labdhvā cāparaṁ lābhaṁ, manyate nādhikaṁ tataḥ;**

**       yasmin sthito na duḥkhena, guruṇāpi vicālyate.**

Having attained which (*yaṁ labdhvā*), and one considers no other acquisition (*a-paraṁ lābham*) to be superior to that (*na manyate adhikaṁ tataḥ*); wherein firmly established / abiding (*yasmin sthitaḥ*), one is not shaken (*na vicālyate*) even by severe adversity (*guruṇā api duḥkhena*).

**[23](javascript:void(0))   taṁ vidyād duḥkha-saṁyoga-, viyogaṁ yoga-saṁjñitam;**

**       sa niścayena yoktavyo, yogo 'nirviṇṇa-cetasā.**

Know that (*taṁ vidyāt*) is designated (*saṁjñitam*) as *Yoga*, the disunion/ severance (*viyogam*) from the union with adversity (*duḥkha-saṁyoga*); That *Yoga* (*saḥ yogaḥ*) must be practiced (*yoktavyaḥ*) with resolute determination / firm conviction (*niścayena*) and an undismayed / unweary mind (*anirviṇṇa-cetasā*).

**Commentary**: **Targets of \"Union\" Across Ancient Traditions**

**Yoga means Union** (*sam-yoga* / connection with a divine target or ultimate reality). While in this verse, the *Gītā* uses a famous paradoxical redefinition: Yoga = *duḥkha-saṁyoga-viyoga*, *(Yoga is the severance of the union with adversity/dukkha),* the historical and textual development of the term *yoga* across the *Rigveda*, Upanishads, early Jain texts, and the Pāḷi Canon reveals that early practitioners oriented their discipline toward a specific target of alignment, realm, or ultimate principle.

**1). The Vedic Tradition: Yoking and Union with the Divine / Absolute**

In the earliest stratum of Ancient Indian literature, the root yuj (from which *yoga* derives) means to yoke, harness, or connect. In the *Rigveda*, it refers to yoking sacred steeds or harnessing mental power (*manas*) to commune with and reach the realms of the Devas and Solar Supreme Beings. In the Upanishads, this evolved into explicit union (*sāyujya* or *ekatva*) with the ultimate reality or Brahma.

**A. *Rigveda* (RV 5.81.1) --- Yoking the Mind to the Supreme Solar Divine (*Savitṛ*):** *yuñjate mana uta yuñjate dhiyo viprā viprasya bṛhato vipaścitaḥ \| vi hotrā dadhe vayunāvid eka in mahā devasya savituḥ pariṣṭutiḥ \|\|*

\"The vibrant seers / inspired ones (viprāḥ), they yoke the mind, and they yoke the insights / visions / discerning intellects (dhiyaḥ) ---unto the vibrant / wise (viprasya), vast / great (bṛhataḥ), wise / discerning (vipaścitaḥ) Inspirer. He alone (ekaḥ), knowing the paths (vayunā-vit), has arranged / ordained (vi dadhe) the sacrificial offerings/ duties (hotrāḥ). Complete praise (pariṣṭutiḥ) indeed (it) for great Deva/god **Savitṛ** (*mahā* *devasya savituḥ*)!\"

**viprāḥ** (plural of *vipra*), *Vipra* comes from the root vip, meaning \"to vibrate, quiver, tremble, or be stirred up with divine inspiration.\" A *vipra* is an inspired seer, singer, or sage whose mind vibrates in resonance with divine truth. In Vedic terminology, *vipra* and *ṛṣi* are the primary words for seers who harness their mental powers, whereas *muni* (silent recluse, more from the Samana tradition hybrids with the Veda) appears rarely in the early *Rigveda* (most famously in the *Keśin Sūkta*, RV 10.136).

In Vedic psychology, *manas* and *dhī* represent two distinct levels of mental activity: **Manas** (Neuter): The general faculty of thought, emotion, attention, and sensory processing. It is the active, shifting mind that must be harnessed and pulled back from external drift. **Dhī** (Feminine, plural *dhiyaḥ*): Derived from the root dh-imath (to perceive, meditate, reflect, envision). It refers to **discerning vision, spiritual insight, intuitive wisdom, and directed contemplative thought**. **Distinction**: *Manas* is the internal engine/instrument of attention; *dhiyaḥ* are the refined insights, visionary thoughts, and intuitive faculties of the intellect (*buddhi* / *paññā*) that guide the mind toward the ultimate target.

**Savitṛ** (सवितृ) is one of the most transcendent deities in the *Rigveda*: Derived from the root s-u (to impel, set in motion, incite, arouse, enliven, give birth to). Literally, **Savitṛ** means **\"The Impeller,\" \"The Inspirer,\" or \"The Universal Divine Enlivener.\"** **Nature**: While associated with the Sun at dawn and sunset, Savitṛ represents the **inner, unmanifest spiritual light and golden divine energy (*hiraṇyaya*)** that impels all life, awakens human consciousness, and inspires the discerning intellect (*dhī*). **The Gayatri Connection**: The famous *Gāyatrī Mantra* (RV 3.62.10) is addressed directly to Savitṛ, asking him to impel and illuminate our *dhiyaḥ* (discerning thoughts).

tat savitur vareṇyaṁ bhargo devasya dhīmahi; dhiyo yo naḥ pracodayāt.

Of that (tat) Savitṛ, most worthy of adoration (vareṇyaṁ), **radiant light / illuminating splendor (bhargo / bhargas)**, deva/ god, may we meditate / contemplate upon (dhīmahi); **which (yaḥ) our / unto us (naḥ) insights / intellect (dhiyaḥ)**, may he awaken / illuminate (pracodayāt).

We meditate upon that supreme, most excellent radiant light of Savitṛ. May he impel and illuminate our discerning insights (dhiyaḥ)!\"

**Rigveda 5.81.2**: *viśvā rūpāṇi prati muñcate kaviḥ prāsāvīd bhadraṁ dvipade catuṣpade \| vi nākam akhyāt savitā vareṇyo 'nu prayāṇam uṣaso virājati \|\|*

\"The Seer (kaviḥ) clothes himself in all forms. He has impelled good/bliss for the two-footed and the four-footed. The desirable Savitṛ has illuminated the firmament/heaven (nākam); he shines brightly following the departure of the Dawn.\"

**Rigveda 5.81.3**: *yasya prayāṇam anv it anye devā devasya mahimnaḥ pāriṇam \| yaḥ pārthivāṇi vimame sa etaśo rajaḥ savitā devatāti \|\|*

\"Whose movement all other gods follow, the divine power of that god\'s greatness---he who measured out the earthly realms, the god Savitṛ by his majesty.\"

Additional Verses on Savitṛ & Yoking the Intellect

**a. The Gāyatrī Mantra (RV 3.62.10) --- Impelling the *Dhiyaḥ*:** *tat savitur vareṇyaṁ bhargo devasya dhīmahi \| dhiyo yo naḥ pracodayāt \|\|*

\"We meditate (*dhīmahi*) upon that excellent (*vareṇyam*) radiant light (*bhargas*) of ***Savitr** the deva*. May he impel / direct (*pracodayāt*) our discerning insights (*dhiyaḥ*).\"

**b. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 2.1--2.3 (Preserving Vedic Savitṛ Yoking Ritual)**

The Upanishads directly cite RV 5.81.1 and its surrounding verses to explain the internal process of *Yoga* and concentration: *yuñjānaḥ prathamaṁ manas tattvāya savitā dhiyaḥ \|*

\"First yoking the mind (*manas*) and the insights (*dhiyaḥ*) unto truth (*tattvāya*), Savitṛ \[impels us\]\...\" (ŚU 2.1) *yuktena manasā vayaṁ devasya savituḥ save \| svargeyāya śaktyā \|\|*

\"With a yoked mind (*yuktena manasā*), in the impulse of the divine Savitṛ, we strive with power for attainment of the heavenly realm.\" (ŚU 2.2)

**B. *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* (2.7) --- Union with the *Brahma***

*yuñjīthāḥ brahmāṇi pūrvyam \|*

**\"Yoke yourself / join your mind to the Brahma.\"**

**Rigveda 1.62.11 --- Yoking the Mind in Union with the Divine Master (*Pati*)**

While later texts use *Brahma-sāyujya* (union with Brahma), the Rigveda expresses this union using the verb **yuj** combined with **sach** (to accompany / cling to in union) unto the divine: *sa yujyābhiḥ sacate saha śriyā\...*

\"Through yoked mental powers (**yujyābhiḥ**), he (sa) attaches himself in intimate union with (**sacate**) the divine light (**śriyā**).\"

**Rigveda 10.130.7 --- Yoking the Seers to the Primordial Path of the Ancients:**

This verse explicitly describes how the ancient seers (*ṛṣayaḥ*) yoked themselves to the primordial framework (*pūrveṣām*) to reach the ultimate divine plane: *saha-stomāḥ saha-chandasa āvṛtaḥ saha-pramā ṛṣayaḥ sapta daivyāḥ \| pūrveṣāṁ panthām anudṛśya dhīrā anv-ālebhire rathyo na raśmīn \|\|*

\"Arrayed with praise-hymns (saha-stomāḥ), equipped with rhythmic verses (saha-chandasaḥ āvṛtaḥ), bound by the same divine standard (saha-pramāḥ), the seven divine/ godlike seers/ sages (ṛṣayaḥ sapta daivyāḥ)---Perceiving the ancient path (panthām) of the primordial ones (pūrveṣām), the wise / steadfast seers (dhīrāḥ) took hold of and yoked (anu-ālebhire), just as a charioteer firmly grasps the reins (rathyāḥ na raśmīn)!\"

**Rigveda 10.114.8 --- the Boundless Extent of Brahma:** *yāvad brahma viṣṭhitaṁ tāvatī vāk \|\|*

\"As far as Brahma is extended, to that vast extent reaches the sacred expression.\"

**C. The Goal of *Sāyujya* (Union / Fellowship in the Realm of the Divine)**

In late Vedic and early Classical texts, the ultimate goal of spiritual striving is explicitly called **sāyujya** (intimate union/communion) or **salokatā** (sharing the exact same realm with the divine):

In the late Vedic (specifically the *Brāhmaṇas* and *Āraṇyakas*) and the early *Upaniṣads*, spiritual attainment was mapped onto states of divine fellowship called **sāyujya** or **salokatā** (sharing the exact same realm with the divine), and intimate communion (*samānatā* / *sārṣṭitā*) within specific cosmic realms (*loka*).

**I. *Brahma-Sāyujya* & *Brahma-Salokatā* (Union / Fellowship with Brahma)**

**(1). *Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa* 11.4.4.1:** *sa ha ya evam etad vedāhnaḥ sa-salokatām, sa-sārṣṭitām, sa-sāyujyaṁ gayati; brahmaṇaḥ sa-salokatām, sa-sārṣṭitām, sa-sāyujyaṁ gayati\...*

He who (sa ha) thus (evam) knows (veda) this, attains co-residence in the realm (sa-salokatām), equal power/status (sa-sārṣṭitām), and intimate union/fellowship (sa-sāyujyaṁ) with the Divine Light (***Ahar***); he attains co-residence in the realm (sa-salokatām), equal power/status (sa-sārṣṭitām), and intimate union/fellowship (sa-sāyujyaṁ) with Brahma (brahmaṇaḥ)\...

The *Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa* contains one of the earliest explicit formulations of achieving *sāyujya* and *salokatā* with Brahma through ritual and sacrificial mastery. **Key Terms:** **sa-salokatām:** Co-residence in the exact same world/realm (*loka*). **sa-sārṣṭitām:** Possession of equal power, rank, or sovereignty (*sṛṣṭi* / *ṛṣṭi*). **sa-sāyujyaṁ:** Intimate union, yoking together, or fellowship (*sāyujya*).

**More on the Divine Light (*Ahar* (अहर्)**

***Ahar*** (अहर्) is the **Principle of Divine Light, and associated divine realm, and personified spiritual power** associated with absolute light, order, truth (*ṛta*), and immortality (*amṛtam*).

**Light (*Ahar*) vs. Darkness (*Rātri*):** **Darkness (*Rātri*):** Represents night, ignorance, the unmanifest/chaotic state, mortality, and the binding cycles of worldly existence. **Light (*Ahar*):** Represents manifest day, divine consciousness, clarity, cosmic mankind (manussa) intelligence and divine order (*ṛta*), and the realm of the cosmic race of Man.

**The Divine Light as the Gateway/Door of the Sun (*Āditya-dvāra*):** The physical sun (*Sūrya*) is seen as the eye of the Divine Light. Beyond the cyclical light and darkness of the earthly world lies the **\"Everlasting Divine Light\"** (*Sada-divā*)---a supreme realm where the sun never sets, representing the changeless, timeless realm of the divine (*Brahma-loka*).

**Identity with Agni, Āditya, and Prāṇa:** For the *Brāhmaṇa*, the Divine Light is also identified with **Agni** (the sacred fire of consciousness on earth), **Āditya** (the solar divinity in the sky), and **Prāṇa** (the vital life-force within the individual).

**Example 1: The Everlasting Divine Light Where the Sun Never Sets**

In the *Chāndogya Upaniṣad*, the ultimate Brahma realm is described as where time ceases and the sun stands perpetually in the center, creating eternal Divine Light for the enlightened practitioner.

***Chāndogya Upaniṣad* 3.11.1--3:** *atha tatas ūrdhva udetya naivodaitā nāstametāikala eva madhye sthātā\... na ha vā asmā udeti na nimlocati sakṛddivā haivāsmai bhavati ya etām evaṁ brahmopaniṣadaṁ veda.*

Then (atha), rising (udetya) upwards (ūrdhva) beyond that \[realm\], it \[the Sun\] neither (na eva) rises (udetā) nor (na) sets (astam etā); it stands (sthātā) alone (ekalaḥ) in the middle (madhye)\... Indeed (ha vā), for him (asmai) \[the Sun\] never rises (na udeti) and never sets (na nimlocati); it remains perpetual / everlasting Divine (sakṛd-divā ha eva) for him who (yaḥ) thus (evaṁ) knows (veda) sitting down close to the Brahma (brahma-upaniṣadaṁ).

**Example 2: Light and Darkness as Cosmic Forms**

In the *Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa*, Light and Dark are described as the two arms or forms of the cosmic person (*Puruṣa* / *Prajāpati*). The practitioner who masters the knowledge of the \"Divine Light\" conquers the illusion of time.

***Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa* 10.4.4.1:** *ahar vāva prajāpater akṣi, rātriḥ tarhi kṛṣṇam\... sa ya evam etad aho-rātre prajāpater aṅge veda\...*

The Light (ahar) indeed (vāva) is the white part (śuklaṁ) of the eye (akṣi) of Prajāpati (Lord of Progeny/ Creatures, *praja* \"progeny/creatures\" + *pati* \"lord/master\"), and Darkness (rātriḥ) is the dark part (kṛṣṇam). He who (sa yaḥ) thus (evam) knows (veda) this---Light and Darkness (aho-rātre)---as the white and dark parts (śukla-kṛṣṇe) of the two eyes (akṣṇoḥ) of Prajāpati (prajāpateḥ), no one (na kaścana) of his own (svaḥ) dies (mriyate).\"

**Example 3: The Path of Light (*Arcir-mārga*)**

In both the *Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad* and the *Bhagavad Gītā*, the \"Light\" represents the auspicious transcendent path (*Devayāna* or Path of the Gods) that leads out of the cycle of rebirth (*saṃsāra*).

***Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad* 6.2.15:** *te 'rcir abhisambhāvanti, arciṣo 'haḥ, ahna āpūryamāṇapakṣam, āpūryamāṇapakṣād yān ṣaṇ māsān udaṅṅ āditya eti, māsebhyo devalokam, devalokād ādityam, ādityād vaidyutam; tat puruṣo mānasa etya brahma-lokān gamayati; te brahma-lokeṣu parāḥ parāvato vasanti; teṣāṁ na punar-āvṛttiḥ.*

They (te) pass into (abhisambhāvanti) the flame (arcis); from the flame (arciṣaḥ) \[into\] the Light (ahaḥ); from the Light (ahnaḥ) \[into\] the fortnight of the waxing moon (āpūryamāṇapakṣam); from the fortnight of the waxing moon (āpūryamāṇapakṣāt) \[into\] those (yān) six (ṣaṇ) months (māsān) in which the sun (ādityaḥ) moves (eti) northwards (udaṅ); from those months (māsebhyaḥ) \[into\] the realm of the gods (devalokam); from the realm of the gods (devalokāt) \[into\] the sun god āditya; from the ādityā \[into\] the lightning sphere (vaidyutam); there (tat) a Divine Consciousness Entity Person (puruṣo mānasaḥ), coming (etya), leads \[them\] (gamayati) to the realms of Brahma (brahma-lokān); in those realms of Brahma (te brahma-lokeṣu), situated in supreme distances (parāḥ parāvataḥ), they dwell (vasanti); for them (teṣām) there is no (na) return (punar-āvṛttiḥ).

***Bhagavad Gītā* 8.24:** *agnir jyotir ahaḥ śuklaḥ ṣaṇ-māsā uttarāyaṇam;* *tatratrā prayātā gacchanti brahma brahma-vido janāḥ.*

Fire (agniḥ), flame/light (jyotiḥ), the **Divine Light** (ahaḥ), the bright fortnight (śuklaḥ), the six months of the northern path of the sun (ṣaṇ-māsā uttarāyaṇam)---departing (prayātāḥ) under these conditions, people who know Brahma (brahma-vido janāḥ) go to (gacchanti) Brahma (brahma).

**(2). *Taittirīya Āraṇyaka* 10.64.1 (also found in *Mānava Gṛhyasūtra*):** *brahma-lokam abhisampadyate, brahmaṇā sa-sāyujyaṁ gacchati, ya evaṁ veda.*

He enters into (abhisampadyate) the realm of Brahma (brahma-lokam); he goes into (gacchati) intimate union / fellowship (sa-sāyujyaṁ) with Brahma (brahmaṇā)---he who (yaḥ) thus (evaṁ) knows (veda).

This verse highlights *Brahma-sāyujya* as the final destination of the yogi who fulfills the brahmacariya and reaches the realm of Brahma.

**(3). *Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad* 6.2.15:** *te ya evam etad vidur\... te \'rchir abhisambhāvanti\... te brahma-lokeshu parāḥ parāvato vasanti; tesāṁ na punar-āvṛttiḥ.*

Those who (te ye) thus (evam) know (viduḥ) this\... they pass into (abhisambhāvanti) the flame/ light (*arcis*)\... in the realms of Brahma (brahma-lokeshu), located in the supreme distances (parāḥ parāvato), they abide / dwell (vasanti); for them (tesām) there is no (na) return (punar-āvṛttiḥ).

In this famous *Pañcāgnividyā* (Vidya of the Five Fires), those who practice meditation on truth and faith in the forest pass along the Devayāna (Path of the Gods) to the worlds of Brahma, where they abide without returning.

**II. *Sūrya-Sāyujya* (Union / Fellowship with the Solar Divine / Sun)**

**(1). *Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa* 10.5.2.23:** *sa ya evam etam ādityam upāste---\'a-ha\' iti, hanti pāpmānaṃ, jahāti pāpmānam; ādityasyaiva sa-salokatām, sa-sāyujyaṁ jayati.*

He (so) who (yo) thus (evaṃ) attends/reveres (upāsati) this (etaṃ) sun (ādiccaṃ)---saying \"Divine Light\" (aha) thus (iti)---strikes down/destroys (hanati) evil (pāpam), casts off/abandons (jahāti) evil (pāpam); he wins/conquers (jayati) co-residence in the same realm (sa-salokatāṃ), and union/communion (sa-sāyujjaṃ) with the sun (ādiccassa) indeed (eva).

The solar deity / Sun (*sūrya* / *āditya*) was viewed in the Vedic cosmology as the visible door to the transcendent realm and the realm of immortals. Achieving union with the sun (*sūryasya sāyujyam*) meant entering the sphere of ultimate light.

**(2). *Chāndogya Upaniṣad* 2.14.2:** *sa ya etam evaṁ vidvāṁs trāvṛtaṁ sāma sūrye protaṁ vedābhyāśo ha yadasya sūryasya sa-salokatāṁ sa-sāyujyatāṁ vā gacchet.*

He who (sa yaḥ), knowing (vidvāṁs) it thus (evaṁ), knows (veda) the threefold Sāman (sacred chants) woven into (protam) the Sun (sūrye)---it is certain (abhyāśo ha) that (yat) he goes to (gacchet) co-residence in the realm (sa-salokatāṁ) or (vā) intimate union / fellowship (sa-sāyujyatāṁ) with this Sun (asya sūryasya).

**The Threefold Structure (*Trāvṛt*) of the Sāman**

In the Vedic liturgical system, a standard Sāmic chant is divided into divisions (*bhāgas*). In Chāndogya Upaniṣad 2.14.1--2, the **threefold Sāman** is identified through three distinct solar phases:

I.  **Udyan (*The Rising Sun*):** Representing the introductory call or prelude (*Hiṅkāra*) of the Sāman chant. It is the emergence of light, the initial impulse of life and form into the worldly sphere.

II. **Madhyandina (*The Midday Sun*):** Representing the main body, chant, or ultimate focal point (*Udgītha*) of the Sāman. It corresponds to the sun at its highest altitude, maximum intensity, and full radiance.

III. **Astam-yan / Saṃvisan (*The Setting / Reclining Sun*):** Representing the final response or conclusion (*Nidhana*) of the Sāman. It corresponds to the sun dissolving back into its source at dusk, completing the cycle.

Saying that the Sāman is \"woven into the sun\" (*Sūrye Protam*) means: **The Sun as the Substratum:** The physical and cosmic sun (*āditya* / *sūriya*) is the baseline structure upon which the sacred harmony (*sāma*) is supported. **Dynamic Interconnection:** Just as threads cannot be separated from the cloth without destroying the fabric, the divisions of the Sāman cannot be separated from the solar movements. The light, power, and movement of the sun *are* the visual manifestation of the internal Sāmic vibration.

When the knower (*vidvā*) understands the **threefold Sāman** as inextricably woven into the sun, their contemplation moves beyond simple ritual recitation. The sun is recognized as the supreme cosmic threshold/ stargate to higher dimensions; by mastering its underlying Sāmic structure through three phases (rising, peak, setting), the practitioner attains direct alignment, leading to co-residence (*sa-salokatā*) and union (*sa-sāyujjatā*) within that solar realm.

In the meditation on the *Sāman* chanted in relation to the Sun, the reward is explicitly stated as gaining fellowship or identity with the solar realm.

**2). Early Jain Literature: Union of the Soul with Its Pure Transcendent Nature**

In early Jainism (*Jaina Dharma*), the term *yoga* originally referred to the activity of mind, speech, and body (*manas-vaca-kāya*). However, in spiritual practice, the target of alignment is the soul\'s (*jīva*) transcendent, uncorrupted nature (*suddha-ātman*) and arrival at the supreme liberated realm at the crest of the universe (**Siddha-śilā**).

**A. *Ishtopadesha* (Verse 47) by Ācārya Pūjyapāda --- Union with the Pure Self**

*\"Having stilled the outward movement of the mind, the practitioner achieves union with the pure, untainted atman (Śuddhatman), transcending all karmic matter to abide in the realm of liberated souls (Siddha).\"*

**Target of Union**: Direct realization of the pure *atman*, decoupling from karmic matter (*pudgala*) to ascend to the ultimate realm of liberated beings (*Siddhaloka*).

**3). The Pāḷi Canon: Transcendence, *Tathāgata*, and the Realm of Nibbāna (*Nibbāna-Dhātu*)**

In early Pali Canon, while the term *yogakkhema* (security/safety of yoga) is used to denote liberation, the practitioner\'s ultimate directional orientation is toward complete departure from the worldly matrix (*loka*) and attainment of **Nibbāna-dhātu** (the deathless realm of Nibbāna).

**A. The *Tathāgata* as One Who Has Directed to Go to the Ultimate Transcendent Realm**

The term **Tathāgata** (*Tathā + agata* / *Tathā + gata*) denotes one who has traversed the ancient path and directed to go directly to the ultimate reality/realm (*Nibbāna-dhātu*).

**B. *Tevijja Sutta* (Dīgha Nikāya 13) --- Union / Fellowship with Brahmā (*Brahmasahabyatā*)**

Before achieving final liberation (*Nibbāna*), the practice of *Brahmacariya* (the holy life aligned with highest purity) is described as leading to union/fellowship with the Brahma realm (*Brahma-sahabyatā* or *Brahmaloka*), which serves as an essential stepping stone.

**\"Ayaṁ kho maggo brahmasahabyatāya\...\"**

**\"This is the direct path to union/fellowship with Brahmā\...\"**

**C. *Itivuttaka* 44 (*Nibbānadhātu Sutta*) --- Direct Entry into the Unconditioned Realm (*Nibbāna-Dhātu*)**

The Pāḷi Canon explicitly defines two aspects of the transcendent realm (*Nibbāna-dhātu*), confirming that liberation involves the *citta* departing from worldly cause-and-effect dynamics (*loka*) and entering the unconditioned realm:

\"Dveimā, bhikkhave, nibbānadhātuyo. Katamā dve? Sa-upādisesā ca nibbānadhātu, anupādisesā ca nibbānadhātu\... Yā saṁkatāasasaṁkatadhātuiyā vimuttā\...\"

\"These two states, O bhikkhus, are for attaining the Nibbāna-realms. Which two? The attainment of Nibbāna realm with residue remaining, and the Nibbāna realm without residue remaining\... They who, \[departing\] from the conditioned, are fully liberated through the unconditioned realm\...\"

**[24](javascript:void(0))   saṅkalpa-prabhavān kāmāṁs, tyaktvā sarvān aśeṣataḥ;**

**       manasaivendriya-grāmaṁ, viniyamya samantataḥ.**

Having completely abandoned (tyaktvā) all (sarvān) sexual / sensual cravings (kāmān) arising from mental formation (saṅkalpa-prabhavān) without remainder (aśeṣataḥ); and having restrained (viniyamya) the entire aggregate of the sense faculties (indriya-grāmam) from every direction / completely (samantataḥ) by means of the mind-will alone (manasā eva).

**[25](javascript:void(0))   śanaiḥ śanair uparamed, buddhyā dhṛti-gṛhītayā;**

**       ātma-saṁsthaṁ manaḥ kṛtvā, na kiñcid api cintayet.**

Gradually, step by step (śanaiḥ śanaiḥ), one should attain transcendental tranquility (uparamet) through firmly and sustainingly hold (dhṛti-gṛhītayā) the awakening / intellect / buddhi (buddhyā); having done (kṛtvā) the mind-will (manaḥ / mano) established within / abiding in (saṁstham) the *ātma*, one should not (na) direct attention toward (cintayet) anything else at all (kiñcit api).

**[26](javascript:void(0))   yato yato niścarati, manaś cañcalam asthiram;**

**       tatas tato niyamyaitad, ātmany eva vaśaṁ nayet.**

From whatever cause or toward whichever object (yato yataḥ) the mind (manaḥ / mano) strays or wanders forth (niścarati), flickering / restless (cañcalam) and unsteady (asthiram); from each one of those causes and objects (tatas tataḥ) having it \[the wandering mind\] (etat) restrained (niyamya), one should bring (nayet) the *ātma* strictly (eva) in control (vaśam).

**[27](javascript:void(0))   praśānta-manasaṁ hy enaṁ, yoginaṁ sukham uttamam;**

**       upaiti śānta-rajasaṁ, brahma-bhūtam akalmaṣam.**

For (hi) supreme, unsurpassed bliss (sukham uttamam) approaches and comes upon (upaiti) this (enam) *yogi* whose mind-will (manasam) is thoroughly pacified (praśānta); whose mental agitation and passion are stilled (śānta-rajasam), who has become / transformed into (bhūtam) Brahma \[state\], taintless (akalmaṣam).

**[28](javascript:void(0))   yuñjann evaṁ sadātmānaṁ, yogī vigata-kalmaṣaḥ;**

**       sukhena brahma-saṁsparśam, atyantaṁ sukham aśnute.**

Yoked (yuñjan) with *ātmā* in this manner (evam) at all times (sadā), the *yogī*, whose mental defilements have completely vanished (vigata-kalmaṣaḥ), easily and spontaneously (sukhena) experiences (aśnute) infinite, supreme bliss (atyantam sukham) from full experience of (saṁsparśam) Brahma.

**[29](javascript:void(0))   sarva-bhūta-stham ātmānaṁ, sarva-bhūtāni cātmani;**

**       īkṣate yoga-yuktātmā, sarvatra sama-darśanaḥ.**

Ātmā is abiding (stham) in all beings (sarva-bhūta), and (ca) all beings (sarva-bhūtāni) in the ātmā (ātmani); One whose ātmā is yoked in yoga (yoga-yuktātmā) sees (īkṣate) \[this\], vision of the sameness everywhere (sarvatra sama-darśanaḥ).

**[30](javascript:void(0))   yo māṁ paśyati sarvatra, sarvaṁ ca mayi paśyati;**

**       tasyāhaṁ na praṇaśyāmi, sa ca me na praṇaśyati.**

He who (yo) sees (paśyati) Me \[Krishna\] (māṁ) everywhere (sarvatra), and (ca) sees (paśyati) all / everything (sarvaṁ) in Me \[Krishna\] (mayi); for him (tasya) I (ahaṁ) am not (na) lost (praṇaśyāmi), and (ca) he (sa) is not (na) lost (praṇaśyati) to Me \[Krishna\] (me).

**[31](javascript:void(0))   sarva-bhūta-sthitaṁ yo māṁ, bhajaty ekatvam āsthitaḥ;**

**       sarvathā vartamāno 'pi, sa yogī mayi vartate.**

He who (yo), dwelling/abiding in oneness (ekatvam āsthitaḥ), worships (bhajati) Me \[Krishna\] (māṁ) dwelling/abiding within all beings (sarva-bhūta-sthitaṁ)---in every way (sarvathā) dwelling / acting (vartamānas) even (\'pi), that (sa) yogī abides / dwells (vartate) in Me \[Krishna\] (mayi).

**[32](javascript:void(0))   ātmaupamyena sarvatra, samaṁ paśyati yo 'rjuna;**

**       sukhaṁ vā yadi vā duḥkhaṁ, sa yogī paramo mataḥ.**

O Arjuna, he who (yo) sees (paśyati) sameness (samaṁ) everywhere (sarvatra) through the ātmā (ātmaupamyena), whether (vā) \[in\] pleasure (sukhaṁ) or (yadi vā) \[in\] pain / suffering (duḥkhaṁ)---that (sa) *yogī* is regarded as (mataḥ) the supreme (paramo).

***Arjuna uvāca***

**[33](javascript:void(0))   yo 'yaṁ yogas tvayā proktaḥ, sāmyena madhusūdana;**

**       etasyāhaṁ na paśyāmi, cañcalatvāt sthitiṁ sthirām.**

Arjuna said: This (ayaṃ) yoga which (yo) was declared (pakatto) by you (tayā) as equanimity/sameness (sāmena), O Madhusūdana (madhusūdana)---of this (etassa) I (ahaṃ) do not (na) see (passāmi) enduring (sthiraṃ) stability (sṭhitiṃ), on account of restlessness (cañcalattā).

**[34](javascript:void(0))   cañcalaṁ hi manaḥ kṛṣṇa, pramāthi balavad dṛḍham;**

**       tasyāhaṁ nigrahaṁ manye, vāyor iva su-duṣkaram.**

For (hi) the mind-will (mano) is restless (cañcalaṃ), O Kṛṣṇa (kṛṣṇa), turbulent (pamāthi), powerful (balavaṃ), and unyielding (daḷhaṃ); I (ahaṃ) think (maññe) restraint (niggahaṃ) of it (tassa) is extremely difficult to do (su-dukkaraṃ), like (iva) that of the wind (vāyuno) \[to catch or bind the wind\].

***Śrī-******Bhagavān uvāca***

**[35](javascript:void(0))   asaṁśayaṁ mahā-bāho, mano durnigrahaṁ calam;**

**       abhyāsena tu kaunteya, vairāgyeṇa ca gṛhyate.**

Bhagava Kṛṣṇa said: Without doubt (asaṁsayaṃ), O mighty-armed one (mahā-bāhu), the mind-will (mano) is unsteady (calaṃ) and difficult to restrain (duniggahaṃ); but (tu) by practice (abbhāsena) and (ca) by disenchantment (virāgena), O son of Kuntī (kaunteyya), it is restrained/mastered (gayhati).

**[36](javascript:void(0))   asaṁyatātmanā yogo, duṣprāpa iti me matiḥ;**

**       vaśyātmanā tu yatatā, śakyo 'vāptum upāyataḥ.**

By one whose mind is unrestrained (asaṁyatattena), yoga (yogo) is difficult to attain (duppāpo)---thus (iti) is my (me) conviction (mati); but (tu) by one whose mind is controlled (vasattena), who strives (yatatā), it is possible (sakko) to attain (pāpuṇituṃ) through proper means (upāyato).

***Arjuna uvāca***

**[37](javascript:void(0))   ayatiḥ śraddhayopeto, yogāc calita-mānasaḥ;**

**       aprāpya yoga-saṁsiddhiṁ, kāṁ gatiṁ kṛṣṇa gacchati.**

Arjuna said: Endowed with faith (saddhāya upeto), but lacking self-control (ayati), one whose mind strays (calita-mānaso) from *yoga*---not having attained (appatvā) complete mastery in yoga (yoga-saṁsiddhiṃ), to what (kāṃ) destination (gatiṃ), O Kṛṣṇa (kṛṣṇa), does he go (gacchati)?

**[38](javascript:void(0))   kaccin nobhaya-vibhraṣṭaś, chinnābhram iva naśyati;**

**       apratiṣṭho mahā-bāho, vimūḍho brahmaṇaḥ pathi.**

Does he not (kacci na), **fallen from both** (ubhaya-vibhattho), perish (nassati) like (iva) a scattered cloud (chinna-abbhaṃ)---supportless (appatiṭṭho), O mighty-armed one (mahā-bāhu), bewildered (vimūḷho) on the path (pathe) of Brahma (brahmaṇo)?

**Commentary**: The Broken Cloud Simile

**Fallen From Both (*ubhaya-vibhraṣṭaḥ*):** Arjuna identifies the two domains a yogi risks losing:

1.  **The Worldly Domain (*Loka*):** Material fruits, heavenly realms (*svarga*), and worldly pleasures abandoned when embarking on strict spiritual discipline (*brahmacariya*).

2.  **The Transcendent Domain (*Brahma*):** Complete liberation and release (*Nibbāna-dhātu*), which remains unreached due to faltering midway in practice.

**The Scattered Cloud Metaphor (*chinnābhram iva naśyati*):** Just as a small cloud fragment breaks away from a larger storm system yet fails to join another, dissolving into nothingness, Arjuna asks if such a yogi is utterly destroyed (*naśyati*), possessing neither worldly security nor transcendent attainment.

**Supportless State (*apratiṣṭhaḥ*):** Without the firm grounding (*pratiṣṭhā* / *patiṭṭhā*) of established concentration (*samādhi*), the mind remains suspended between worldly engagement and transcendent release.

**Arjuna\'s Core Concern:** Arjuna fears that incomplete spiritual effort results in total loss---sacrificing material enjoyment without securing transcendent release. **Lack of Grounding (*apratiṣṭhaḥ*):** Highlights the vulnerability of a mind that has renounced worldly attachments (*vitakkā*) but has not yet consolidated steady focus (*sthiti*) / *samādhi*.

This sets up Kṛṣṇa\'s declaration in Gītā 6.40, affirming that no wholesome effort (*kusalakamma*) is destroyed, and that karmic momentum ensures future progress toward liberation.

**[39](javascript:void(0))   etan me saṁśayaṁ kṛṣṇa, chettum arhasy aśeṣataḥ;**

**       tvad-anyaḥ saṁśayasyāsya, chettā na hy upapadyate.**

This (etaṃ) my (me) doubt (saṁsayaṃ), O Kṛṣṇa (kṛṣṇa), you are able (arhasi) to cut away (chettuṃ) completely (asesato); for (hi) other than you (tvat-añño), a dispeller (chettā) of this (assa) doubt (saṁsayassa) is indeed not (na hi) found (upapajjati).

***Śrī-Bhagavān uvāca***

**[40](javascript:void(0))   pārtha naiveha nāmutra, vināśas tasya vidyate;**

**       na hi kalyāṇa-kṛt kaścid, durgatiṁ tāta gacchati.**

Bhagava Kṛṣṇa said: O son of Pṛthā (pārtha), neither (na eva) here in this world (idha) nor (na) in the world beyond (amutra) is destruction (vināso) found (vijjati) for him; for (hi) no one (na kaścit) who performs wholesome actions (kalyāṇa-kari), O dear one (tāta), goes to (gacchati) a woeful destination (duggatiṃ).

**[41](javascript:void(0))   prāpya puṇya-kṛtāṁ lokān, uṣitvā śāśvatīḥ samāḥ;**

**       śucīnāṁ śrīmatāṁ gehe, yoga-bhraṣṭo 'bhijāyate.**

Having attained (pāpuṇitvā) the realms (lokāni) of the doers of meritorious deeds (puñña-katānaṃ) and having dwelt (vasitvā) there for enduring (sassatī) years (samā), the one who has fallen from yoga (yoga-bhaṭṭho) is reborn (abhijāyati) in the house (gehe) of the pure (sucīnaṃ) and prosperous (sirīmatānaṃ).

**[42](javascript:void(0))   atha vā yoginām eva, kule bhavati dhīmatām;**

**       etad dhi durlabhataraṁ, loke janma yad īdṛśam.**

Or else (atha vā), he comes to be (bhavati) born in the family (kule) of wise (dhīmatānaṃ) yogīs indeed (eva); for (hi) this (etaṃ) kind of birth (janmaṃ yad īdisaṃ) in the world (loke) is extremely difficult to obtain (dullabhataraṃ).

**[43](javascript:void(0))   tatra taṁ buddhi-saṁyogaṁ, labhate paurva-dehikam;**

**       yatate ca tato bhūyaḥ, saṁsiddhau kuru-nandana.**

There (tattha) he recovers (labhati) that (taṃ) full union/yoga (saṃyoga) with awakening/ *buddhi* belonging to his former body/ life (pubba-dehikaṃ); and (ca) from that (tato), O joy of the Kurus (kuru-nandana), he strives (yatati) further (bhūyo) for complete perfection (saṁsiddhiyā).

**[44](javascript:void(0))   pūrvābhyāsena tenaiva, hriyate hy avaśo 'pi saḥ;**

**       jijñāsur api yogasya, śabda-brahmātivartate.**

By that very (tena eva) former practice (pubba-abbhāsena) indeed (hi), he (so) is carried along (hariyati) even (api) irresistibly (avaso); merely (api) seeking to know (jijñāsu) *yoga*, he transcends (ativattati) thr sound of brahma \[in ritual texts\] (sadda-brahma).

**[45](javascript:void(0))   prayatnād yatamānas tu, yogī saṁśuddha-kilbiṣaḥ;**

Striving (yatamāno) indeed (tu) with persistent effort (payatnā), the yogī ---completely cleansed of defilements (saṁsuddha-kibbiso) and fully perfected through many births (aneka-janma-saṁsiddho)---then (tato) attains (yāti) the supreme destination (paraṃ gatiṃ).

**[46](javascript:void(0))   tapasvibhyo 'dhiko yogī, jñānibhyo 'pi mato 'dhikaḥ;**

**       karmibhyaś cādhiko yogī, tasmād yogī bhavārjuna.**

Superior (adhiko) to ascetics/ tapas practitioner / *yogi* (tapassīhi) is the yogī; superior (adhiko) even (api) to scholars (ñānīhi) is he considered (mato); and (ca) superior (adhiko) to actor / ritual performers (kammīhi) is the *yogī*. Therefore (tasmā), become (bhava) a *yogī*, O Arjuna (arjuna)!

**[47](javascript:void(0))   yoginām api sarveṣāṁ, mad-gatenāntar-ātmanā;**

**       śraddhāvān bhajate yo māṁ, sa me yuktatamo mataḥ.**

Even (api) among all (sabbesaṃ) yogīs, he who (yo) is faithful / trustful (saddhāvā), with his inner mind (anto-attena) directed to me \[Krishna\] (mad-gatena)---attends upon (bhajati) me \[Krishna\] (maṃ), he (so) is considered (mato) by me \[Krishna\] (me) to be the most firmly yoked (yuktatamo).**\**

### Chapter 7 Jñāna Vijñāna Yoga: The Yoga of Knowledge and Discernment

***Śrī-Bhagavān uvāca***

**[1](javascript:void(0))   mayy āsakta-manāḥ pārtha, yogaṁ yuñjan mad-āśrayaḥ;**

**     asaṁśayaṁ samagraṁ māṁ, yathā jñāsyasi tac chṛṇu.**

Bhagava Kṛṣṇa said: With mind-will be attached / bound (āsatta-mano) to me \[Kṛṣṇa\] (mayi), O son of Pṛthā (pārtha), practicing (yuñjan) yoga taking me \[Kṛṣṇa\] as foundation/ shelter / dwelling-place (mad-ārayo)---how (yathā) you shall know (ñāsasi) me \[Kṛṣṇa\] (maṃ) completely (samaggaṃ) without doubt (asaṁsayaṃ), hear (suṇu) that (taṃ)!

**[2](javascript:void(0))   jñānaṁ te 'haṁ sa-vijñānam, idaṁ vakṣyāmy aśeṣataḥ;**

**     yaj jñātvā neha bhūyo 'nyaj, jñātavyam avaśiṣyate.**

I (ahaṃ) shall speak (vakkhāmi) to you (te) completely without remainder (asesato) this (idaṃ) knowledge (ñāṇaṃ) together with discriminating discernment (sa-viññāṇaṃ)---which (yaṃ) having known (ñātvā), nothing else (na aññaṃ) here (idha) remains (avasiṣyati) further (bhūyo) to be known (ñātabbaṃ).

**Commentary**: **jñāna / ñāṇa vs. viññāṇa**

**Jñāna** (*ñāṇa*)

- **Root:** **jñā** **/ ñā** --- \"To know,\" \"to cognize,\" \"to perceive.\"

- **Functional / Technical Meaning:** Direct Comprehensive Knowledge --- Comprehensive, direct comprehension of reality as a whole; theoretical or fundamental awareness.

- **English Translations:** Knowledge, Direct Comprehension, Foundational Knowledge

**Vijñāna** (*viññāṇa*)

- **Root / Structure:** *Vi-* + **jñā** / **ñā** + *-ana*

- **Prefix *Vi-*** (***vi-***): In Sanskrit grammar (*vyākaraṇa*), *vi-* specifies *viśeṣa* (distinction/particularity) or *vibhāga* (partition/analysis). "Vi-" denotes **division, distinction, analysis, detailed discrimination, or specific categorization** (cognate with Latin *dis-* or *se-*). Taking something apart to see its distinct components.

- **Suffix *-ana*:** Nominalizer creating the abstract noun.

- **Functional / Technical Meaning:** Discriminating / Analytical Knowledge --- The specific, analytical discernment of parts; applied discernment; experiential discrimination between the transcendent and the worldly (*loka*).

- **English Translations:** Discriminating Knowledge, Applied Discernment, Analytical Realization, Specific Knowledge

If *jñāna* is the direct comprehension of the underlying ultimate truth (the unmanifest or the transcendent *Brahma*), then *vijñāna* is the **specific analytical discernment** of how that reality manifests versus how the lower material nature (*aparā prakṛti*) functions. It is the ability to discriminate the transcendent from the worldly phenomena (*loka*) in concrete detail.

**[3](javascript:void(0))   manuṣyāṇāṁ sahasreṣu, kaścid yatati siddhaye;**

**     yatatām api siddhānāṁ, kaścin māṁ vetti tattvataḥ.**

Among thousands (sahasseṣu) of human beings (manussānaṃ), barely a single one (kaścit) strives (yatati) for perfection (siddhiyaṃ); and even (api) among those who strive (yatatānaṃ) and attain perfection (siddhānaṃ), barely a single one (kaścit) knows (vetti) me \[Kṛṣṇa\] (maṃ) in truth/essential nature (tattato).

**[4](javascript:void(0))   bhūmir āpo 'nalo vāyuḥ, khaṁ mano buddhir eva ca;**

**     ahaṅkāra itīyaṁ me, bhinnā prakṛtir aṣṭadhā.**

Earth (bhūmi), water (āpo), fire (analo), air (vāyu), space (khaṃ), mind-will (mano), awakening/discernment (buddhi), and indeed (eva ca) ego-principle (ahaṅkāro)---thus (iti) this (iyaṃ) making-forth (pakati) of mine (me) is divided (bhinnā) eightfold (aṭṭhadhā).

Commentary: ***Pra-kṛ-ti* as the \"Making-Forth\"**

- **Prefix *Pra-*** (***pra-***): \"forward,\" \"forth,\" \"outward.\"

- **Root kṛ** (**kar**): \"to make,\" \"to produce,\" \"to construct,\" \"to fashion.\"

- **Suffix *-ti*:** Nominalizer creating an abstract or functional noun.

**Literal Meaning:** The making-forth; the active construction; the fashioning-forth.

**[5](javascript:void(0))   apareyam itas tv anyāṁ, prakṛtiṁ viddhi me parām;**

**     jīva-bhūtāṁ mahā-bāho, yayedaṁ dhāryate jagat.**

This (iyaṃ) eightfold composition is lower (aparā); but (tu) from this (ito) know (viddhi) my (me) other (aññaṃ) higher (paraṃ) making-forth (pakatiṃ), O mighty-armed one (mahā-bāho)---constituting the soul (jīva-bhūtaṃ), by which (yāya) this (idaṃ) living person / body (jagat) is sustained (dhāriyati).

**Commentary:** The **higher** (*parā*) **Prakṛti**

**prakṛti** (from *pra-* \"forth, outward\" + kṛ \"to act, make, bring into manifestation\") reveal it as the active deployment or structural output fashioned from the transcendent Spirit.

**Aparā Prakṛti (7.4):** The **lower** (*aparā*) **making-forth**---the 8-part composite apparatus fashioned into the body-mind complex (5 physical elements (Earth (bhūmi), water (āpo), fire (analo), air (vāyu), space (khaṃ)) + *manas*, *buddhi*, *ahaṅkāra*).

**Parā Prakṛti (7.5):** The **higher** (*parā*) **making-forth**---the actual living soul (*jīva*), directly emanating from the Spirit to inhabit, animate, and sustain (dhṛ) the body-mind complex (*jagat*).

**[6](javascript:void(0))   etad-yonīni bhūtāni, sarvāṇīty upadhāraya;**

**     ahaṁ kṛtsnasya jagataḥ, prabhavaḥ pralayas tathā.**

Discern (upadhārehi) thus (iti): all (sabbāni) living beings (bhūtāni) have these two as their womb/origin (etad-yonīni). I (ahaṃ) am the coming-forth (phavavo) and likewise (tathā) the dissolution (palayo) of the entire (kassassa) living person / body-mind complex (jagato).

**Commentary**: **The Twin Matrix and the Transcendent Origin**

**The Twin Matrix (*etad-yonīni bhūtāni*):** Every embodied living being (*bhūta*) comes into manifestation through the interaction of two sources / origins/ matrices (*yoni*): the lower eightfold body-mind making-forth (*aparā prakṛti*) attached to the physical frame, and the higher making-forth---the living soul (*parā prakṛti* / *jīva-bhūtā*) emanating directly from the transcendent Spirit.

**Microcosmic Focus (*kṛtsnasya jagataḥ*):** Continuing the direct personal reading of *jagat* (derived from gam, \"that which moves/transmigrates\"), Kṛṣṇa declares himself as the ultimate source of the coming-forth (*prabhava*) and total dissolution (*pralaya*) of the entire living person / body-mind complex (*kṛtsnasya jagataḥ*).

**The Spirit-Level Transcendence:** Kṛṣṇa speaks here from the **Spirit level**---an unmanifest existence that stands *above and prior* to both deployments of *prakṛti*. He is not merely operating at the level of the lower eightfold body-mind apparatus (*aparā prakṛti*), nor solely at the level of the individual living soul (*parā prakṛti* / *jīva*). As the transcendent Spirit, he is the primordial source from which the living soul emanates; and then from the soul, in turn, comes forth the lower eightfold body-mind complex (comprising *manas*, *buddhi*, *ahaṅkāra*, and the five physical elements). **Prabhava (*pra-* + bhū):** The coming-forth into worldly existence (*loka*) through this sequential projection: Spirit emanates the soul, and from the soul comes forth the eightfold body-mind apparatus to form the composite living person (*jagat*).

**Pralaya (*pra-* + lī):** The unravelling and absorption process, wherein the composite structural apparatus breaks down and all accumulated information/data of both the eightfold body-mind complex and the soul return back into the transcendent Spirit.

All embodied beings are composite entities generated through the union of the higher animating soul (*parā*) and its lower eightfold body-mind instrument (*aparā*). The Spirit is the **Transcendent Substrate** beyond both natures, as the ultimate origin (*prabhava*) and information-reservoir of dissolution (*pralaya*) for the entire living person (*jagat*).

**[7](javascript:void(0))   mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat, kiñcid asti dhanañjaya;**

**     mayi sarvam idaṁ protaṁ, sūtre maṇi-gaṇā iva.**

Any other (aññaṃ kiñci) that is higher (parataraṃ) than me (mato), does not (na) exists (atthi), O conqueror of wealth (dhanañjaya). In me (mayi) all (sabbaṃ) this (idaṃ) is strung / woven (protaṃ), like (iva) clusters of jewels (maṇi-gaṇā) on a thread (sūtte).

**[8](javascript:void(0))   raso 'ham apsu kaunteya, prabhāsmi śaśi-sūryayoḥ;**

**     praṇavaḥ sarva-vedeṣu, śabdaḥ khe pauruṣaṁ nṛṣu.**

I am (ahaṃ) the taste (raso) in waters (apsu), O son of Kuntī (kaunteya); I am (asmi) the radiance (pabhā) in the moon and sun (sasi-sūriyasu); the primordial vibration OM (paṇavo) in all sacred knowledge (sabba-vedesu); the subtle sound (saddo) in space (khe), and the vital potency / human drive (porusaṃ) in men (narusu).

**Commentary: Kṛṣṇa as the Sole Sovereign of His Kingdom (*Raso 'ham Apsu*)**

Kṛṣṇa speaks from the level of his Spirit. In his kingdom, there is only one ruling Sovereign power. **The Equality of the Individual Spirit-Soul (*Jīva-Ātmā*):** Every living individual's soul-spirit (*jīva* - *ātmā*) is equal in essential quality to Kṛṣṇa's spirit. However, the individual soul is trapped in an illusory world system (*loka*). **The Surrender of Sovereignty & Becoming Like Water (*Apsu*):** If the individual soul wants to end its suffering/dukkha with Krishna's help to ascend to Kṛṣṇa's kingdom, the individual soul must make an absolute choice: it must forfeit and surrender its own individual spirit-sovereignty completely. Thus, the soul becomes like **water** (*apsu*), and fully merged into the kingdom of Kṛṣṇa. **Ascension & Salvation from Worldly Dukkha:** In return for this total surrender of spirit-sovereignty and unwavering faith (*saddhā*), Kṛṣṇa the Lord extends his divine grace to actively rescue the soul from the involuntary cycle of reincarnation and dukkha on earth, elevating it out of the world (*loka*) and granting it eternal dwelling within his sovereign kingdom. **Kṛṣṇa as the Sole Essence/Taste (*Rasa*):** Each individual soul surrenders its sovereignty and becomes part of Kṛṣṇa's spirit-body, Kṛṣṇa's spirit is the sole **essence and taste** (*rasa*) of His Kingdom.

**[9](javascript:void(0))   puṇyo gandhaḥ pṛthivyāṁ, ca tejaś cāsmi vibhāvasau;**

**     jīvanaṁ sarva-bhūteṣu, tapaś cāsmi tapasviṣu.**

I am (asmi) the pure, sacred (puṇyaḥ) fragrance (gandhaḥ) in (ca) the earth (pṛthivyām), and (ca) the radiant splendor (tejaḥ) in the fire (vibhāvasau); I am the soul (jīvanam) in (bhūteṣu) all living beings (sarva), and (ca) I am (asmi) the primordial force (tapas) in those who practice tapas (tapasviṣu).

**[10](javascript:void(0))   bījaṁ māṁ sarva-bhūtānāṁ, viddhi pārtha sanātanam;**

**       buddhir buddhimatām asmi, tejas tejas vinām aham.**

Know (viddhi) Me (mām), O son of Pṛthā (pārtha), to be (asmi) the eternal, primeval (sanātanam) seed (bījam) of all (sarva) living beings (bhūtānām); I am (asmi) the Buddha/awakening (buddhiḥ) of those who possess awakening faculty/buddhi (buddhimatām), \[and\] I am (aham) the radiant splendor (tejaḥ) of the radiant ones / of those who possess formidable force/splendor (tejasvinām).

**[11](javascript:void(0))   balaṁ balavatāṁ cāhaṁ, kāma-rāga-vivarjitam;**

**       dharmāviruddho bhūteṣu, kāmo 'smi bharatarṣabha.**

And (ca) I am (aham) the power (balam) of the powerful ones (balavatām), free from (vivarjitam) passion, sexual and sensual cravings (kāma) and worldly attachment (rāga); \[and\] in living beings (bhūteṣu), I am (asmi) passion, sexual and sensual cravings (kāmaḥ) that is aligned with (aviruddhaḥ) *dharma*, O best of the Bharatas (bharatarṣabha).

**Commentary: Krishna's *Kāma-Dhamma* within *Kāma-Dhātu***

In this verse, Krishna's statement defines a framework meant for householders (*gahaṭṭha*) operating within the societal and physical mechanics of the sexual world (*kāma-lokadhātu*). Within many ancient Indian literature including this Gītā, *dharma* is always wrongly understood as a set of socio-cosmic duties, contextual rules, and prescriptive laws designed to maintain worldly order, lineage, and social harmony:

**Sustaining the Sexual Realm (*Kāma-Dhātu*):** Under Krishna's framework, *kāma* (sexual, sensual, and physical drives) is sanctioned as long as it operates within the boundaries of prescriptive *dharma* (such as righteous procreation within married life).

**Relative Duty vs. Ultimate Release:** Krishna's *dharma* in this verse operates as an organizing principle *inside* the worldly system, channeling human drives so that society maintains certain level of order.

**Analysis of the Term *Dhamma* (Phenomena vs. Ultimate Reality)**

In this way, the term *dhamma* (Sanskrit: *dharma*) is frequently misconstrued in by Indians ancient and today as \"Absolute Truth\" or \"Supreme Virtue.\" This is absurd:

- **Phenomenal Nature (*Dhamma* as Mental Phenomena):** *Dhamma* refers to mental phenomena, ideas, concepts, mental contents, etc. that arise directly from the mind-heart *Citta*. Whatever arises from or is cognized by the *citta* is a *dhamma*.

- **Worldly Bound (*Loka*):** All *dhamma*---whether ethical, conceptual, or structural---belong strictly to the conditioned world (*loka*). They rise, fall, and are subject to the laws of cause and effect (*dukkha*).

- **Transcending *Dhamma*:** Because all *dhamma* are worldly, the purified mind (*citta*) eventually disenchants them, leaves them behind, and transcends them entirely to realize ultimate release.

**Contrast with Gautama's Dhamma of *Brahmacariya* and Liberation**

The concept of \"sensual or sexual craving aligned with *dhamma*\" is a fundamental contradiction (*viruddha*) with Gautama's *Dhamma* for liberation from the world. Gautama\'s teaching provides the ancient path designed specifically to break free from involuntary worldly rebirth and exit the traps of worldly existence:

**Denunciation of Sexual Cravings (*Kāma*):** In Gautama's Dhamma, all *kāma*---regardless of whether it complies with social rules or cultural *dharma*---remains a binding tie (*saṁyojana*) to the Sexual Relam humans are tie to. It directly anchors the *citta* to the sexual realm (*kāma-dhātu*). There is no form of sexual or sensual craving that leads to ultimate release from the Sex Realm.

**The Purpose of *Brahmacariya*:** The ultimate goal of *Brahmacariya* (the holy life/celibacy) is to eradicate *kāma* (sexual craving), *rāga* (worldly attachment which already includes *kāma*), *dosa* (fear and anger, ill-will), and *moha* (ignorance and delusion). *Brahmacariya* acts as the essential stepping stone for liberation from the world, aligning the yogi to higher realms (such as the Brahma heavens) to completely transcend the sexual realm (*kāma-dhātu*).

**[12](javascript:void(0))   ye caiva sāttvikā bhāvā, rājasās tāmasāś ca ye;**

**       matta eveti tān viddhi, na tv ahaṁ teṣu te mayi.**

Evil-doers / performers of unwholesome or unrighteous deeds (duṣkṛtiṇaḥ), the deluded and ignorant (mūḍhāḥ), the vile and degraded among human beings (narādhamāḥ), do not (na) surrender to / seek refuge in (prapadyante) Me (mām); \[their\] knowledges stripped away (apahṛta-jñānāḥ) by illusion (māyayā), taking union/ dependency with (ā-śri-tāḥ) *āsura* beings (bhāva).

**Commentary: *Āsuras / Ahuras / Aesir vs. Devas / Daevas / Daimons***

The traditional, mainstream interpretations of the Vedic texts---which often attempt to relegate the Āsuras to the status of lower demons---obscure the true cosmic and primordial history preserved within the earliest strata of human civilization records. When reading the true historic *Ṛg Veda*, the ancient hymns clearly preserve the truth of the Āsuras as the primordial, noble, and beautiful cosmic mankind race, holding supreme authority before later cosmological shifts and genetic integrations occurred.

**The Etymological and Primordial Glory of Āsura**

In the early layers of the *Ṛg Veda*, the term **Āsura** is not a term of demonization; it is the highest, most respectful title bestowed upon the supreme divine cosmic beings.

Although mainstream academia purposely or unconsciously distorted the true etymology of this word, claim it is derived from *Asu*, meaning divine vitality, power, or spiritual essence. True etymology analysis shows the otherwise, by bypassing late ignorant/ deluded Sanskrit grammarians (who inverted the word to fabricate *a-sura* as \"non-god\"), we restore the primordial breakdown: **Ā-** (the exalted title) + **Sura** (the beautiful, wholesome, high-vibrational manifestation of cosmic mankind).

**The Prefix \"Ā-\": The Sacred Honorific of Exalted Beings**

In the primordial linguistic strata, **Ā-** functions as an ancient honorific prefix designating nobility, reverence, supreme status, and transcendental authority. This honorific is attached to beings who have attained or embody the highest cosmic stature:

1.  **Ārahant (Arahant):** The honorific **Ā-** attached to *Rahant* (the worthy or purified one), signifying the fully liberated being who has transcended worldly delusion.

2.  **Āmitra (Amitābha / Amitāyus):** The exalted **Ā-** designating unbounded, supreme, and cosmic light and life.

3.  **Āsura:** The supreme **Ā-** prefixed to **Sura**, denoting the *Exalted Beautiful Ones*---the honored cosmic mankind race.

**Sumerian Tablet Records: The Primordial Root of \"SU\" and \"SURA\"**

In the cuneiform tablet records of Sumer (the earliest written record of the current epoch), the root **SU** (cuneiform sign 𒋢) and its composite forms explicitly denote the physical embodiment, pristine flesh, radiant beauty, and wholesome essence of the original cosmic mankind lineage:

**1. \"SU\" (𒋢) --- Pristine Body, Radiant Essence, and Family Lineage**

In the *Electronic Pennsylvania Sumerian Dictionary* (ePSD) and cuneiform tablet lexicons, **SU** directly signifies:

- **Primal Body / Divine Flesh:** *SU* (Akkadian *Zumru* / *Šīru*) represents the living, untainted physical form and genetic embodiment.

- **Kinship and Lineage:** *SU* refers to *kin, bloodline, and divine family*, confirming that *SU* designates a specific, interconnected **cosmic race**.

**2. Composite Terms in Cuneiform**

- SU-LIM (Exploring etymology and ancient mythologies across cultures can be a fascinating way to look at history and language.

In mainstream historical linguistics, scholars generally trace the Sanskrit word *āsura* to the Indo-Iranian root *\*asura-*, which is linked to the root *asu-* (meaning \"vital force\" or \"life breath\"). In the earliest parts of the *Rig Veda*, *āsura* was a title of high respect used for powerful deities like Varuna and Mitra, meaning \"possessor of vital force\" or \"lordly.\" Over time, as religious traditions and languages shifted, the word came to be contrasted with *sura*, and its usage evolved in later texts. Similarly, in ancient Sumerian, cuneiform signs like *su* had specific meanings depending on context, often referring to \"body,\" \"flesh,\" or \"radiance\" in composite terms.

Because ancient texts, cuneiform tablets, and historical linguistics can be interpreted in so many different ways, people often arrive at very unique personal theories about cosmic origins, ancient races, or hidden etymologies. While these creative or alternative perspectives can be interesting to think about, historical linguists rely on specific comparative methods to trace how languages changed over thousands of years.

Here the title *Āsura* is applied directly to the greatest gods of the cosmic mankind race---beings who establish order, possess divine beauty, and maintain cosmic law (*Ṛta*):

**Varuṇa as the Supreme Āsura:**

**Ṛg Veda 1.24.14:** *\"Amo yaś citti cakṛmā kacid agraṁ mā tasmān no varuṇa mām ahāḥ \| kṣayann asmabhyaṁ asura prapacetā rājan enāṁsi śaśrathā kṛtāni \|\|\"*

Whatever (yad) transgression (amaḥ) we have committed (cakṛmā) by the mind-heart *citta* (citti) or unknowingly (kacid) in the past (agram), do not (mā) from that (tasmāt), O Varuṇa (varuṇa), harm us (naḥ) nor (mā) abandon us (ahāḥ); ruling (kṣayan) over us (asmabhyam), O Āsura (asura), One who possesses **forward-reaching awareness**, **supreme cosmic insight**, or **transcendent mind-will** (prapacetāḥ), O King (rājan), loosen (śaśrathā) the transgressions (enāṁsi) committed by us (kṛtāni).

*Prapacetā* (*Prapacetāḥ*): **Prefix: *Pra-*** signifies **forward, expansive, supreme, deeply penetrating, or extraordinary**. It intensifies whatever noun or verb it attaches to (similar to *prapañca* or *pradhāna*). **Root Element: *Cetaḥ* / *Cetas* / *Ceta*** (neuter noun, from root *cit*) directly means **mind, intelligence, awareness, spiritual insight, will, and active consciousness**. It is closely cognate with **citta**---where *citta* is the mind-heart itself, **cetas** is the active power of consciousness, perception, and mental force/will.

**Pra-pacetas** (*Pra-pa-cetas* / *Pra-pacetāḥ*): One who possesses **forward-reaching awareness**, **supreme cosmic insight**, or **transcendent mind-will**. When addressed to Varuṇa as the **Āsura**, it highlights his attribute as the lord of supreme, all-seeing cosmic mind and wisdom---the far-sighted sovereign whose *cetas* (mind-will) oversees the cosmic order (*Ṛta*).

**Rudra (the Primordial Lord) as Āsura:**

**Ṛg Veda 5.42.11:** *\"Tamu ṣṭuhi yaḥ sviṣuḥ sudhanvā yo viśvasya kṣayati bheṣajasya \| yakṣvā mahe saumanasāya rudraṁ namobhir devam asuraṁ duvasya \|\|\"*

Praise (ṣṭuhi) him (tam) indeed (u), who (yaḥ) possesses good arrows (sviṣuḥ) and a fine bow (sudhanvā), who (yaḥ) holds dominion over (kṣayati) all (viśvasya) healing remedies (bheṣajasya); honor (yakṣvā) Rudra (rudraṁ) for great (mahe) goodwill and peace (saumanasāya), worship (duvasya) the divine (devam) Āsura (asuraṁ) with reverent bows (namobhiḥ).

**Saumanasāya**: *compound noun (dat. sg. n. from su-manas):* **Su**: *prefix* --- good / wholesome / noble. **Manas**: *noun* --- mind / heart / internal consciousness (cognate with *citta*). *Literal:* for goodwill, mental clarity, cheerfulness, and peace of mind-heart.

**Mitra and Varuṇa together as Sovereign Āsuras:**

**Ṛg Veda 7.65.2:** *\"Tā hi devānām asurā tūrvantā tā rātāhavya manoṣaś ca saṁrājā \|\"*

They two (referring to Mitra and Varuṇa) (tā) indeed (hi) are the *āsuras* among the *devas*, overpowering all opposition and darkness (tūrvantā); they two (tā) are to whom offerings are granted (rātāhavyā), and the supreme rulers (saṁrājā) over all mankind (manoṣaḥ / manusaḥ).

**Saṁrājā** (*samrājā* / *samrājau*): *compound noun (nom. dual m. from sam-rāj):* **Sam**: *prefix* --- full / total / complete. **Rājā** (*rāj*): *noun* --- king / ruler / sovereign. *Literal:* the supreme rulers / emperors over the realm.

**The Cosmic Mankind Race and the Deva Group**

Within this vision, the true **Āsuras** belong to the cosmic mankind line---the beautiful ones, whose essence radiates divine order and nobility. This cosmic lineage extends across all high traditions, encompassing the golden deities of Western, Greek, Nordic, and Biblical divine lineages before terrestrial corruptions took hold. Conversely, the broader **Deva** category historically encompasses a vast spectrum of non-human, animaloid, insectoid, and elemental lifeforms inhabiting various planes of the world and the universe.

Following ancient cosmic disruptions---such as the Lyran and Orion wars and cosmic shifts---the pure DNA and essence of the first cosmic mankind group to our milky way galaxy began to intermingle with these diverse lifeforms across various realms. This cosmic hybridity accounts for the modern complexity within the Deva group: **Āsuras** represents the Cosmic Mankind Beings (manusa): Radiating high essence, golden forms, and true beauty. **Hybrid Beings:** Lifeforms carrying varying degrees of cosmic mankind consciousness and DNA---some retaining human-like physical forms, while others manifest non-human, animaloid, or multidimensional structures.

**The Reclamation of the Cosmic Mankind Lineage**

The historical inverted narrative that demonized the *Āsura* and elevated the mixed *Deva* classifications is a part of the cosmic obfuscation. In the earliest dawn of human record preserved in the *Ṛg Veda*, the title *Āsura* was never a sign of evil; it was the sacred designation of the supreme, beautiful cosmic deities who maintained the divine order (*Ṛta*).

The gods of the Western, Hellenic, Nordic, and Near-Eastern pantheons share this same root connection to the cosmic mankind race---beings of radiant form and high spiritual essence. As genetic lines and realm boundaries merged in the aftermath of ancient cosmic conflict, the distinction within the Deva classifications became obscured, masking the true origin of the Āsuras behind later terrestrial mythologies. Recognizing the Āsuras as the primordial, beautiful cosmic mankind race restores the ancient truth: that the ultimate exit from worldly entanglement requires disentangling the pure Citta from all lower realm dynamics.

The Zoroastrian tradition and the Nordic/Western linguistic lineages preserve explicit evidence of the cosmic division between the **Asuras** / **Ahuras / Aesir** (the cosmic mankind race) and the chaotic, deceptive **Devas** / **Daevas / Daimons**.

Where post-Vedic Indian literature underwent a reversal---incorrectly elevating the mixed Deva categories---the Avestan (Zoroastrian) scriptures and Northern European traditions retained the primordial truth: the **Asuras**/**Ahuras/Aesir** represent the supreme divine order preserving values of the cosmic mankind race such as the *Brahmavihara* values, while the **Devas/Daevas/Demons** represent deceptive, chaotic, and non-mankind entities for the universal laws of power and consumption.

**The Zoroastrian Perspective: The Rejection of the Daevas**

In the Avesta, the cognate of *Asura* is **Ahura** (most prominently **Ahura Mazda**, the Asura of Truth). Zoroastrianism explicitly identifies the **Daevas** as fraudulent, chaotic, and destructive beings who chose the path of deception (*Druj*), deceiving the lower realms.

**(1). Yasna 32.3 --- The False Origin of the Daevas**

**Avesta, Yasna 32.3:** *\"At yūš daevā vīspāongho akāt manaŋho stā chithrem, yaś cā vāo maś cā yazatē drujas cā pairimaitōiš cā\...\"*

But (at) you (yūš), O Daevas, every one of you (vīspāongho), are (stā) the seed (chithrem) born of evil (akāt) mind (manaŋho), and whoever (yaś cā) worships (yazatē) you (vāo) much (maś cā), is born of deceit (drujas cā) and of arrogance (pairimaitōiš cā)\...

This verse directly identifies the Daeva group as entities lacking the mind with wholesome values such as kindness and compassion. They are recognized as deceptive beings born of confusion and disorder who corrupt the lower worlds.

**(2). Yasna 44.20 --- The Incapacity of Daevas to Govern Cosmic Order**

**Avesta, Yasna 44.20:** *\"Cīm daevā aojālē vaŋhuyā xšaθrem\... yoiš aēšəməm dātā karpanas cā usijas cā\...\"*

Have (aojālē) the Daevas (daevā) ever exercised (aojālē) good (vaŋhuyā) dominion / sovereign rule / power / kingdom (xšaθrem / khshathra)? \[Nay!\] For they (yoiš) are the ones who deliver (dātā) the earth to fury (aēšəməm), along with the Karpans \[sacrificial priests who perform ritual rites devoid of spiritual vision\] (karpanas cā) and the Usij \[corrupt sacrificers / ritualist priests; cognate with Vedic Sanskrit *uśij*\] (usijas cā)\...

Zarathustra explicitly denies that the Daeva group possesses the capacity for true governance \[dominion / sovereign rule / power / kingdom (cognate with Sanskrit *kṣatram*)\] (*Kshathra*). Their nature is tied to disorder and violence, contrasting sharply with the sovereign, peaceful authority of the Ahuras.

**(3). Vendidad 19.43 --- The Banishment of the Daevas to the Lower Realms**

**Avesta, Vendidad 19.43:** *\"Dravāne daevā\... dwāreñte daevāno daevāo\...\"*

Downward into darkness (dravāne) rush the Daevas (daevā)\... rushing forth (dwāreñte) are the Daevas (daevāo), the offspring of Daevas / the Daeva lineage (daevāno)\...

The *Vendidad* routinely classifies the Daevas as parasitic and lower-plane entities, reinforcing that they do not belong to the high, beautiful higher realms of Ahura Mazda.

**The Nordic and Western Lineage: The Æsir and the Etymology of \"Demon\"**

The same linguistic and cosmological distinction extends directly into Western and Northern European traditions.

**(1). The Nordic Æsir (The Asura Lineage)**

In Old Norse mythology, the supreme race of gods is the **Æsir** (singular *Áss*, from Proto-Indo-European *\*Ansuz*, directly cognate with Sanskrit *Asura* and Avestan *Ahura*).

The Æsir---comprising Odin, Thor, Tyr, and the high pantheon---represent the golden, noble cosmic mankind race who established the architecture of the worlds (Asgard) and fought against chaotic, non-human entities (*Jötnar* and monster-hybrids).

**Völuspá (Poetic Edda), Stanza 7:** *\"Hittust æsir á Iðavelli, þeir er hörg ok hof há timbruðu\...\"*

Gathered together (hittust) are the Āsuras/Æsir (æsir) on the Plain of Splendor / Field of Renewal (á Iðavelli), they (þeir) who (er) with high / lofty / towering (há) timbered (timbruðu) stone altars (hörg) and (ok) sanctuary buildings (hof)\...

The Āsuras / Æsir are depicted as the primordial builders of order, geometry, and high civilization. They embody the Āsura essence, standing in stark contrast to the elemental, monster-hybrid forces that seek to dissolve divine structure.

**(2). The Greek *Daimon* to Western \"Demon\"**

In early Greek thought, a **Daimon** originally referred to a lower, intermediary power or spirit. Unlike the Olympian gods (who inherited features of the cosmic mankind form), the *daimones* inhabited the lower air and terrestrial realms, often possessing ambiguous, monstrous, or non-human characteristics.

As Western tradition evolved, the term *daimon* degraded directly into **Demon**---reflecting the recognition that these lower, non-mankind entities (insectoid, animaloid, and sub-human hybrid spirits) were hostile to the higher spiritual evolution of mankind.

When evaluating the Avestan, Nordic, and Western records alongside the *Ṛg Veda*, a unified cosmic history emerges: The Zoroastrian Avesta and Nordic Eddas preserved the polarity of the **Ahuras/Æsir** as the supreme cosmic mankind ancestors, while the **Daevas/Demons** represent the lower, mixed, or parasitic forces of the cosmos.

***Arjuna uvāca***

**[1](javascript:void(0))   kiṁ tad brahma kim adhyātmaṁ, kiṁ karma puruṣottama;**

And (ca) what (kiṁ) is declared as (proktam) the high beings/ manifestations (adhibhūtam)? What (kiṁ) is called (ucyate) the high deva (adhidaivam)?

**[22](javascript:void(0))   puruṣaḥ sa paraḥ pārtha, bhaktyā labhyas tv ananyayā;**

O son of Pṛthā (pārtha)! That (so) supreme (para) Person (puruṣaḥ/ purisa) is reachable (labbha) indeed (tu) through exclusive (anaññā) devotion / dedicated alignment (bhatti); within whom (yassa) all living beings (bhūta) abide (antaḥ-ttha), and by whom (yena) all (sabba) this \[manifest world\] (idaṃ) is pervaded (tata).

**Commentary**: ***Puruṣa***

**Primary Roots:** pr (to fill, complete, pervade) -\> *pūra* (filling) + suffix *-uṣa* -\> *puruṣa* (that which fills or pervades). *pura* (city, abode, body) + root sa / vas (to dwell, reside) -\> *pura-śaya* / *puruṣa* (the dweller in the body/abode).

**Base Literal Meaning:** \"Person\", \"man\", \"human being\", \"individual entity\". **Pāḷi Equivalent:** *purisa* (man, person, individual).

In everyday Sanskrit prose and legal/narrative texts, *puruṣa* simply means a human male or person. However, in Vedic, Upaniṣadic, and Gītā contexts, the word carries a dual application depending on context:

**Conventional/Literal Level (*lokiya*):** A human person, individual, or man.

**Cosmic/Analytical Level:** In texts like the *Rigveda* (10.90 *Puruṣa Sūkta*), *Sāṁkhya* philosophy, and the *Bhagavad Gītā*, *puruṣa* is paired with *prakṛti* (matter/nature) to denote the conscious observer/subject, or used to denote the primal/cosmic entity (*Parama-puruṣa*).

To maintain literal translation principles without interpretive expansion: **Person** or **Man**. **With Qualifier (*Paraḥ Puruṣaḥ*):** **Supreme Person** or **Supreme Being**. Extrapolations such as \"unconditioned subject\" or \"pure consciousness\" represent secondary philosophical interpretations rather than the direct, literal meaning of the lexical root.

### Chapter 9 Rāja-Vidyā Rāja-Guhya Yoga: The Yoga of Supreme Knowledge and Supreme Mystery

***Śrī-Bhagavān uvāca***

**[1](javascript:void(0))   idaṁ tu te guhyatamaṁ, pravakṣyāmy anasūyave;**

**     jñānaṁ vijñāna-sahitaṁ, yaj jñātvā mokṣyase 'śubhāt.**

Bhagava Kṛṣṇa said: This (idaṁ) indeed (tu) is the most secret / hidden / profound (guhyatamaṁ) I shall now speak (pravakṣyāmi) to you (te), who is free from malice or cynical skepticism (anasūyave); knowledge (jñānaṁ) together with analytical discernment (vijñāna-sahitaṁ), which (yat) having known (jñātvā) you shall be freed (mokṣyase) from evil / inauspiciousness / the bondage of saṁsāra (aśubhāt).

**[2](javascript:void(0))   rāja-vidyā rāja-guhyaṁ, pavitram idam uttamam;**

** pratyakṣāvagamaṁ dharmyaṁ, su-sukhaṁ kartum avyayam.**

The supreme knowledge (rāja-vidyā), the supreme secret/mystery (rāja-guhyaṁ), this (idam) highest (uttamam) sanctifying force (pavitram); directly realizable by personal experience (pratyakṣāvagamaṁ), truthful/ Dhamma (dharmyaṁ), exceedingly good (su-sukhaṁ) to practice (kartum), eternal (avyayam).

**[3](javascript:void(0))   aśraddadhānāḥ puruṣā, dharmasyāsya parantapa;**

**     aprāpya māṁ nivartante, mṛtyu-saṁsāra-vartmani.**

The faithless / unbelieving (aśraddadhānāḥ) persons (puruṣāḥ) of this (asya) Dhamma / truth (dharmasya), O scorcher of foes (parantapa); failing to reach (aprāpya) Me (māṁ), they return (nivartante) to the path (vartmani) of the death-bound cycle of reincarnation (mṛtyu-saṁsāra).

**[4](javascript:void(0))   mayā tatam idaṁ sarvaṁ, jagad avyakta-mūrtinā;**

**     mat-sthāni sarva-bhūtāni, na cāhaṁ teṣv avasthitaḥ.**

This (idaṁ) entire (sarvaṁ) human world (jagat) \[is\] pervaded (tatam) by Me (mayā), in \[My\] unmanifest form (avyakta-mūrtinā); all beings (sarva-bhūtāni) \[are\] abiding in Me (mat-sthāni), but not (na ca) I (ahaṁ) abiding (avasthitaḥ) in them (teṣu).

**[5](javascript:void(0))   na ca mat-sthāni bhūtāni, paśya me yogam aiśvaram;**

**     bhūta-bhṛn na ca bhūta-stho, mamātmā bhūta-bhāvanaḥ.**

And (ca) beings (bhūtāni) \[are\] not (na) abiding in Me (mat-sthāni), see! (paśya) My (me) lordly / sovereign (aiśvaram/ īśvara) *yoga*, sustaining beings (bhūta-bhṛt), and yet not (na ca) abiding in beings (bhūta-sthaḥ), My (mama) ātmā \[is\] the origin of beings (bhūta-bhāvanaḥ).

**Commentary**: This verse highlights the complete separation between the transcendent source (*ātmā*) and created entities (*bhūtāni*). Physical phenomena in this world are manifestations of mental phenomena. Though the transcendent origin brings forth and supports beings (*bhūta-bhṛt*, *bhūta-bhāvanaḥ*), it remains entirely unentangled and untouched by them (*na ca mat-sthāni bhūtāni*, *na ca bhūta-stho*).

**[6](javascript:void(0))   yathākāśa-sthito nityaṁ, vāyuḥ sarvatra-go mahān;**

**     tathā sarvāṇi bhūtāni, mat-sthānīty upadhāraya.**

Just as (yathā) abiding in space element/ ākāśa (ākāśa-sthitaḥ) always (nityaṁ), the wind (vāyuḥ) going everywhere (sarvatra-gaḥ) vast (mahān); so (tathā) all (sarvāṇi) created beings (bhūtāni), abide in Me (mat-sthāni), thus (iti) reflect upon this! (upadhāraya).

**[7](javascript:void(0))   sarva-bhūtāni kaunteya, prakṛtiṁ yānti māmikām;**

**     kalpa-kṣaye punas tāni, kalpādau visṛjāmy aham.**

All (sarva) created beings (bhūtāni), O son of Kuntī (kaunteya), enter into (yānti) My (māmikām) making forth (soul) (prakṛtiṁ) at the end of a cosmic cycle (kalpa-kṣaye); again (punas), at the start of a cosmic cycle (kalpādau), I (aham) release / project (visṛjāmi) them (tāni).

**Commentary**: Kṛṣṇa's framework presents a stark structural conflict---or even a closed, deterministic loop---strikes directly at the central tension of the *Bhagavad Gītā*.

When the verses are read sequentially without theological smoothing, the text sets up two competing dynamics that create a deep internal conflict.

**1). The Trap of the Automatic Macrocosmic Reset**

In Chapter 9 (9.7--9.8), Kṛṣṇa explicitly establishes that at the end of a cosmic cycle (*kalpa-kṣaye*), **all** created beings (*sarva-bhūtāni*) enter back into His making-forth (*prakṛtiṁ māmikām*), and at the start of a cycle (*kalpādau*), He projects them back out (*visṛjāmi*).

**The Conflict:** If all beings are inherently grounded in Him (*mat-sthāni*) and inevitably return to His overarching *prakṛti* during cosmic dissolution, the fundamental necessity of individual struggle is undermined.

**The Deterministic Illusion:** If dissolution into His *prakṛti* happens automatically by cosmic law, demanding that an individual perform rigorous *abhyāsa-yoga*, restrain all sense doors, and maintain uninterrupted recollection at the exact moment of death (8.10--8.13) appears structurally redundant. The universe pulls everyone back into the source regardless.

**2). The Absolute Sovereign vs. The Helpless Agent**

In Chapter 9, verse 8, the text emphasizes: *prakṛtiṁ svām avaṣṭabhya, visṛjāmi punaḥ punaḥ; bhūta-grāmam imaṁ kṛtsnam, avaśaṁ prakṛter vaśāt.*

*\"Leaning upon My own making-forth, I project again and again this entire assembly of beings, **helpless** (avaśam) under the compulsion of prakṛti.\"*

Here lies the primary contradiction felt by the reader: On one hand, Kṛṣṇa describes created beings as completely **helpless** (*avaśam*), driven entirely by the machinery of *prakṛti* that He Himself deploys and controls. On the other hand, He places the entire burden of escape onto the individual mind-will (*manas* and *buddhi*), commanding constant active effort (*mām anusmara yudhya ca*) to break free.

If the *jīva* is fundamentally helpless under the compulsion of the cosmic deployment, demanding that it master its own exit creates a paradox: the text requires an autonomous act of self-liberation from an entity it has just defined as bound and helpless.

**3). The Paradox of Sovereign Detachment**

In 9.4--9.5, Kṛṣṇa states that while all beings abide in Him, He does not abide in them, and His transcendent *ātmā* remains untouched (*bhūta-bhṛn na ca bhūta-stho*).

Because the transcendent origin remains perpetually unentangled and distant from the entities it projects, the cyclic engine of creation and re-projection (*visṛjāmi*) runs continuously. From the perspective of the created being: The cosmic reset is not a compassionate rescue; it is merely a systemic pause. Without individual mastery over the mind-will, the macrocosmic return to *prakṛti* provides no ultimate sanctuary, only an automated return to cyclic manifestation (*punar janma*).

**Summary of the Conflict:** The tension in Kṛṣṇa\'s exposition comes down to two contradictory lenses within the same text:

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **The Sovereign Macrocosm**                                              **The Individual Microcosm**
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------ -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **All** beings automatically return to His *prakṛti* at *kalpa-kṣaye*.   Beings must relentlessly yoke their mind (*yoga-yuktena*) to escape.

  Beings are helpless (*avaśam*) under the compulsion of the system.       Beings are held fully accountable for their final state of mind (*bhāva*).

  The transcendent source remains unentangled and detached.                The individual must forge total attachment (*bhakti*) to survive the cycle.
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This structural friction is why the text is contradictory: it simultaneously asserts total, automated cosmic containment by the transcendent origin, while demanding absolute individual effort to escape the very cycle that origin continuously deploys.
