**Commentary: The Non-Dual Cosmic dissolution vs. The Ariya Dualistic Path of Liberation**

**I. The Non-Dual Framework of the Gītā: The Collapse of Moral Distinctions**

In the Bhagavad Gītā, Krishna's framework operates from an underlying non-dual, cosmic monism. Within a non-dual system, the Krishna's kingdom indifferent to the moral polarities of the manifest world. As expressed earlier in Verse 29 (*samo 'haṁ sarva-bhūteṣu na me dveṣyo 'sti na priyaḥ*), the Supreme holds neither hatred (*dveṣya*) nor favoritism (*priya*), functioning as an impersonal cosmic ground.

Under this non-dual lens:

- **Moral Neutralization:** Good and evil, purity and defilement, virtuous conduct (*sādhu*) and extremely wicked conduct (*su-durācāro*) are treated as temporary, lower-level manifestations belonging to the realm of temporary illusion (*prakṛti* / *māyā*).

- **Dissolution of Ethical Gravity:** Because the ultimate non-dual essence remains identical across all beings, moral distinctions do not possess absolute, unyielding reality. The line between a depraved actor (*su-durācāro*) and a righteous person (*sādhu*) is collapsed simply through a shift in orientation (*ananya-bhāk*), because the underlying cosmic essence makes no fundamental distinction between wholesome and unwholesome actions.

- **The Cosmic Mode:** Operating through power and cosmic play (*līlā*) rather than an absolute Law of Values, this non-dual-ism dissolves ethical boundaries. In doing so, it keeps consciousness bound within the cosmic order (*loka*), sustaining endless worldly becoming (*bhava*) under the guise of an all-inclusive oneness.

**II. Gautama's Ariya Teaching: Liberation Grounded in Absolute Duality**

In direct, uncompromising contrast to cosmic non-dualism, Gautama's authentic Ariya framework affirms that the Noble Mankind race rests on **strict, unyielding Duality**. Liberation is not the realization that \"all is one,\" nor is it the collapse of moral categories. It is the active, clear-sighted realization of fundamental dualistic separations:

**Worldly Realm (Loka):** Unwholesome (*Akusala*), Defilement (*Kilesa*), Sensual Realm (*Kāma-Dhātu*), Bondage to Saṃsāra **VS.**

**World-Transcending (Lokuttara):** Wholesome (*Kusala*), Purity (*Visuddhi*). Brahma Standard / Nibbāna-Dhātu, Complete Liberation

**1). The Operational Framework of Purification**

The dualistic structure is the indispensable foundation of the Law of Values and the entire path of *sīla*, *samādhi*), and *paññā*:

- **Wholesome (*kusala*) vs. Unwholesome (*akusala*):** Evil actions driven by *rāga* (worldly craving and attachment), *dosa* (fear, anger, aversion, hatred), and *moha* (ignorance and delusion) are not superficial labels; they are active, binding defilements that generate direct *kamma* and keep the *citta* enslaved to the cosmic structure.

- **Worldly (*lokiya*) vs. World-Transcending (*lokuttara*):** Unwholesome conduct (*su-durācāra*) is bound strictly to this world (*loka*), which is governed by the laws of decay and death.

**2). The Role of *Brahmacariya* and the Law of Values**

For someone stuck in extreme wickedness (*su-durācāra*), a true turning point cannot occur through a non-dual glossing over of past deeds. It requires an absolute, structural break with the sensual realm (*kāma-dhātu*):

**Cutting *Kāma-Dhātu* via *Brahmacariya*:** *Brahmacariya* (Holy Life) provides the structural foundation for transcending *kāma-dhātu*. By establishing complete celibacy and sensuality restraint, it cuts sexual craving (*taṇhā*) at its root and halts the continuation of becoming (*bhava*).

**The Law of Values via *Brahmavihāra*:** The *citta* is purified not by collapsing good and evil, but by replacing unwholesome states with the Four Brahmanic Abidings (*brahmavihāra*):

- *Mettā* (kindness) directly eliminates hostility (*dosa*).

- *Karuṇā* (compassion) removes cruelty and hardness.

- *Muditā* (Brahmic joy) dismantles lower contraction.

- *Upekkhā* (equanimity/transcendental elevation) stabilizes the *citta*, lifting it beyond worldly agitation toward higher refinement and the Pure Abodes (*Suddhāvāsa*).

**III. Critical Analysis: *Samyag Vyavasito* --- Non-Dual Exemption vs. Dualistic Re-Alignment**

When Verse 30 declares that a person of extremely wicked conduct (*su-durācāro*) can be judged as virtuous (*sādhu*) simply because he has rightly bound his resolve (*samyag vyavasito*) to Krishna, two vastly different interpretations emerge depending on the underlying framework:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Dimension**                       **Non-Dual Cosmic Monism (Gītā Framework)**                                       **Ariya Dualism (Gautama\'s Framework)**
  ----------------------------------- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Status of Evil**                  Relative, superficial (*māyā*); ultimately dissolved in the non-dual reality.     Real, binding defilement (*akusala*); belongs to *loka* and produces *dukkha*.

  **Meaning of *Samyag Vyavasito***   An inner devotional shift toward the non-dual Supreme, overriding past karma.     Absolute re-binding the *citta* to *Sīla*, *Brahmacariya*, and the Law of Values.

  **Mechanism of Liberation**         Realizing identity/devotion with the cosmic deity that transcends good and bad.   Complete disencumbrance, disenchantment, and dualistic separation of *citta* from *loka*.

  **Final State**                     Absorption into the non-dual cosmic kingdom (*prakṛti*).                          Transcendence to *Nibbāna-dhātu*, leaving all worldly *dhamma* behind.
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**       kaunteya pratijānīhi, na me bhaktaḥ praṇaśyati.**

Quickly (kṣipraṁ) his / her ātmā becomes (bhavati) aligned with truth / dhamma (dharmātmā), and eternally (śaśvac) enters into (nigacchati) peace (chāntiṁ); O son of Kuntī (kaunteya), know for certain (pratijānīhi), My (me) devotee (bhaktaḥ) does not (na) perish / get lost (praṇaśyati).

### Chapter 10 Vibhūti Yoga: The Yoga of Divine Manifestations

***Śrī-Bhagavān uvāca***

**[1](javascript:void(0))   bhūya eva mahā-bāho, śṛṇu me paramaṁ vacaḥ;**

**     yat te 'haṁ prīyamāṇāya, vakṣyāmi hita-kāmyayā.**

Bhagava Kṛṣṇa said: Listen again (bhūya eva), O mighty-armed one (mahā-bāho), hear (śṛṇu) my (me) supreme and transcendent (paramaṁ) words (vacaḥ); which (yat) I (aham) shall speak (vakṣyāmi) to you (te), who are beloved and full of joy (prīyamāṇāya), out of a desire for your welfare (hita-kāmyayā).

**Commentary: Aśvattha, The Sacred Fig Tree**

**Aśvattha** directly translates to **\"that under which horses stand\"** or **\"the place where horses are tethered\"**:

Aśvattha = aśva (horse) + stha (standing / staying)

In botanical taxonomy, **Aśvattha** is the exact ancient Sanskrit term for ***Ficus religiosa***, known in English as the **Sacred Fig**, **Peepal**, or **Bodhi Tree**.

**Why is it specifically called \"Sacred Fig\" in English?**

The English common name **\"Sacred Fig\"** is a direct translation of its Latin binomial name, ***Ficus religiosa***: ***Ficus***: The botanical genus comprising all fig plants. ***religiosa***: Meaning *\"sacred,\" \"revered,\"* or *\"pertaining to religious practice.\"*

Botanists and translators gave it this name because no other tree in South Asian history carries as extensive a record of religious veneration across multiple traditions. The sacred status attributed to the *Ficus religiosa* across antiquity rests on four primary historical, cultural, and ecological pillars:

**(1). Pre-Vedic & Harappan Antiquity**

Archaeological discoveries from the Indus Valley Civilization (c. 2500 BCE) reveal steatite seals depicting horned deities standing inside or alongside the foliage of the *Ficus religiosa*. This indicates that veneration of the tree predates formal textual traditions.

**(2). Vedic Sacrificial Fire Rites (*Arani*)**

In Vedic ritual practice (*Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa*, *Taittirīya Saṁhitā*), the *Aśvattha* tree was viewed as the container of latent fire and primordial force (*tapas*). The upper wooden drill-stick used to generate friction and ignite sacred fires (*agnyādhāna*) was harvested from *Aśvattha* wood.

**(3). Site of Awakening & Liberation**

In early Pāḷi literature, Siddhattha Gautama sat beneath an *Assattha* tree (*Ficus religiosa*) at Uruvelā (Bodh Gaya) to liberate himself from the worldly bondage of *rāga* (worldly craving and attachment), *dosa* (fear, anger, aversion and hatred), and *moha* (ignorance and delusion). Because the historical Buddha realized full liberation beneath it, the tree later became known as the **Bodhi Tree** (*Tree of Awakening*) and was preserved as a primary symbol of world-transcending release.

**(4). Unique Botanical & Ecological Traits**

- **Constant Rustling:** The long, attenuated leaf tips (*drip-tips*) cause the foliage to tremble and rustle softly even in near-still air, creating a distinct ambient sound and cool microclimate.

- **Epiphytic Lifespan & Longevity:** *Ficus religiosa* seeds germinate on other structures or rocks, sending down massive root systems that allow individual trees to live for centuries or millennia (such as the *Jaya Sri Maha Bodhi* in Sri Lanka, recorded for over 2,200 years).

**Esoteric & Metaphysical Dimensions of the Aśvattha (Sacred Fig)**

Beyond its botanical traits, ancient esoteric, ritual, and metaphysical traditions attribute deep mystical significance to the *Aśvattha* (*Ficus religiosa*).

**(1). The Inverted Cosmic Tree (*Urdhva-mūlam Adhah-śākham*)**

The most famous esoteric archetype appears in the *Katha Upanishad* (2.3.1) and *Bhagavad Gītā* (15.1--3), where the *Aśvattha* is depicted as an **inverted tree** spanning the dimensions of existence: *Ūrdhva-mūlam adhaḥ-śākham aśvatthaṁ prāhur avyayam*

- **Roots Above (*Ūrdhva-mūlam*):** The primary root originates in the subtle, unseen, unmanifest source of cosmic manifestation.

- **Branches Below (*Adhaḥ-śākham*):** The branches spread downward, representing the physical worlds, sensory objects, elements, and gross physical bodies.

- **The Leaves as Vedic Chants:** The leaves represent worldly knowledge and rituals that sustain cosmic order within the sphere of rebirth (*saṃsāra*).

**Esoteric Meaning:** The physical world experienced through the senses is not the root of reality, but its lower, downward-branching extension. The dynamic, rustling foliage of the *Aśvattha* symbolizes the continuous flow of worldly phenomena springing from subtle cause-and-effect laws.

**(2). The Vessel of Primordial Force & Latent Sacred Fire (*Agni-Garbha*)**

In Vedic esoteric ritualism (*Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa* and *Rigveda*), the *Aśvattha* is designated as *Agni-garbha*---the **womb of latent fire**:

- **Creation of Fire (*Arani*):** When igniting the sacrificial fire, priests took two pieces of wood. The lower block was carved from the *Śamī* tree (representing receptive feminine force), while the upper churning rod (*pramantha*) was carved specifically from *Aśvattha* wood that had grown epiphytically inside a *Śamī* tree.

- **Symbolic Friction:** Churning the *Aśvattha* rod against the *Śamī* block was viewed as drawing unmanifest, latent primordial force (*tapas*) out of subtle space and into physical manifestation as visible flame.

**Esoteric Meaning:** The *Aśvattha* was seen as a living physical conduit capable of storing and conducting unmanifest spiritual and energetic force into the physical realm.

**(3). The Axis Mundi & the Threshold of Awakening (*Bodhimanda*)**

In esoteric geography and ancient cosmological models, the *Aśvattha* functions as an **Axis Mundi**---a central pillar connecting the physical earth with celestial realms:

- **The Immutable Seat (*Bodhimanda*):** In early textual accounts, the ground beneath the *Assattha* at Uruvelā was believed to be the spot on earth capable of supporting the weight of ultimate transcendence.

- **Cosmic Gateway:** It represents the exact physical coordinate where the mundane world touches the unconditioned. Sitting beneath its canopy, the mind turns inward to dismantle the subtle bonds of worldly existence.

**(4). Epiphytic Growth & The Illusion of Worldly Existence**

The unique natural lifecycle of the *Aśvattha* carries intense esoteric symbolism regarding how consciousness becomes entangled in physical matter:

- **Airborne Seeds:** *Aśvattha* seeds are tiny and often carried by birds to land in the crevices of other host trees, stone structures, or buildings.

- **Overgrowing the Host:** The seed sends down thin aerial roots that gradually thicken, weave around the host structure, and eventually encase it entirely.

**Esoteric Meaning:** This process serves as an allegory for how mental phenomena (*citta* and sankhāras) generate subtle worldly clinging and attachment (*upādāna*) that gradually weaves around physical forms, creating the illusion that consciousness is bound to a physical host.

**Comparative Analysis: Sacred Fig (Aśvattha) vs. Sacred Oak**

**Shared Status as World Trees**

Both the **Sacred Fig** (*Ficus religiosa*) and the **Oak** (*Quercus*) occupy parallel metaphysical statuses as paramount \"World Trees\" (*Axis Mundi*). Across geographical zones, ancient civilizations selected these specific trees as physical manifestations of cosmic law, divine order, and thresholds to the unmanifest.

**The Oak Tree as a Cosmic Axis in World Mythologies**

The **Oak tree** (*Quercus*) functions across Indo-European, Celtic, Slavic, and Greco-Roman traditions as a primary **World Tree** (*Axis Mundi*), cosmic pillar, and divine sky-conduit. While Asian traditions prioritized species like the *Ficus religiosa* (*Aśvattha*) or *Banyan*, European and Near Eastern mythologies elevated the massive, long-lived Oak to represent the central structural axis of the cosmos.

![The Oak as a central cosmic pillar, AI generated](media/image4.jpeg){width="2.176388888888889in" height="1.8125in"}**Core Roles of the Oak as a World Tree Across Cultures**

**(1). Slavic Mythology: The World Oak (*Kosmoczny Dąb*)**

In ancient Slavic cosmology, the **Oak** serves as the literal central axis connecting the three realms of existence:

- **Branches (Svarga / Heavens):** Home of **Perun**, the god of thunder, lightning, and sky. The golden eagle sits in its top branches.

- **Trunk (Jav / Physical Earth):** The realm of living human beings, terrestrial beasts, and physical matter.

- **Roots (Nav / Underworld):** Deep beneath the earth, surrounding the roots, lies the domain of **Veles**, the horned god of the underworld, waters, and livestock.

**(2). Celtic & Druidic Traditions: The Pillar of Strength**

- **Etymological Root:** The word *Druid* (*Dru-wid-es*) is philologically linked to the Celtic root for oak (*deru-* / Proto-Indo-European *\*dóru-* \"tree, wood, strong\") combined with *\*weid-* (\"to see / know\"), translating literally to **\"oak-knower\"** or **\"those who possess oak-wisdom.\"**

- **Sacred Groves (*Nemeton*):** Druids held ceremonies within sacred oak groves. The oak was viewed as a living conduit drawing cosmic lightning down into physical soil.

**(3). Norse & Germanic Myth: Yggdrasil & Donar\'s Oak**

- **Yggdrasil\'s Identification:** While the Prose and Poetic Eddas describe **Yggdrasil** (the Norse World Tree that unites the Nine Realms) most commonly as an Ash tree (*Askr Yggdrasils*), historical scholars and regional traditions frequently recorded local Germanic tribes worshiping giant sacred **Oaks** as earthly pillars of Yggdrasil.

- **Donar\'s Oak (*Donareiche*):** In continental Germanic paganism, the massive Oak at Geismar was dedicated to Thor/Donar as the primary physical pillar binding heaven and earth.

**(4). Greco-Roman Tradition: Zeus & the Oracle at Dodona**

- **Zeus / Jupiter\'s Sacred Tree:** The Oak was sacred to Zeus (Jupiter).

- **The Oracle of Dodona:** The oldest Hellenic oracle, Dodona, centered around a massive sacred Oak tree. Priests (*Selloi*) interpreted the rustling of its leaves as the direct voice and divine will of Zeus speaking through cosmic space.

**Esoteric and Mystical Analysis: The Oak and the Sacred Fig**

From an esoteric and mystical perspective, the **Oak** (*Quercus*) and the **Sacred Fig** (*Ficus religiosa* / *Aśvattha*) represent two distinct cosmic archetypal structures that govern how primordial force, consciousness, and physical manifestation interact across the subtle and dense planes of existence.

**(1). Geometric Dynamics: Upward Transmission vs. Downward Emanation**

The primary mystical distinction lies in the direction of energy flow through their subtle geometries.

**The Oak: The Cosmic Lightning Rod (Upward Conduit)**

- **The Vertical Pillar:** Esoterically, the Oak acts as a massive energetic bridge connecting the subterranean chthonic realms with the highest sky spheres. It draws up dense, earthy currents from its deep roots and acts as an antenna for celestial fire.

- **The Conductive Vessel:** In Western esoteric traditions, the Oak's high moisture content and physical density made it a literal target for lightning strikes. Mystically, this represents **the descent of divine grace or celestial force directly into dense physical matter**, igniting the dormant spiritual spark within the earth.

**The Sacred Fig: The Inverted Tree of Emanation (Downward Inversion)**

- **Roots Above, Branches Below (*Ūrdhva-mūlam adhah-śākham*):** Mystically described in ancient esoteric texts (such as the *Kaṭha Upaniṣad* and *Bhagavad Gītā* 15.1), the *Aśvattha* is an inverted cosmic tree. Its root system originates in the unmanifest realm, while its foliage and branches expand downward to form the entire physical and subtle universe (*loka*).

- **The Web of Cause and Effect:** The leaves and aerial roots represent the intricate network of mental formations, karmic cause and effect, and sensory attachments that bind consciousness to worldly existence.

**(2). The Nature of Fire and Primordial Force (*Tapas*)**

Both trees are intimately linked to fire, but their esoteric fire operates through entirely different mechanisms.

- **The Oak and Celestial Fire:** The fire of the Oak is **extrinsic and electric**. It is the violent, sudden flash of sky-fire (the lightning of Zeus, Perun, or Thor) striking from above to break open physical form and purge impurities. It represents sudden illumination, dramatic initiation, and spiritual power breaking through material resistance.

- **The Sacred Fig and Latent Friction-Fire:** The fire of the *Aśvattha* is **intrinsic and latent**. Historically used to craft fire-drills (*araṇī*) for sacred rites, its wood conceals dormant heat. Esoterically, this represents the primordial force (*tapas*) stored secretly within physical matter. It is generated through internal churning, focused concentration (*samādhi*), and intense spiritual effort, transforming raw energy into purified awareness.

**(3). Structural Stability vs. Ephemeral Illusion**

- **The Oak as Material Anchorage:** The Oak embodies strength, endurance, and structural permanence within the physical realm. Mystically, it represents the protective boundary of the cosmic order---holding the world stable against chaos, anchoring human consciousness in duty, virtue (*sīla*), and physical reality.

- **The Aśvattha as Transitory Illusion:** The name *Aśvattha* itself carries the esoteric meaning *a-śvas-tha* (\"that which does not remain the same tomorrow\"). While vast and ancient, its rustling leaves never stay still, representing the incessant fluctuation of worldly phenomena. It is the mystic emblem of *saṃsāra*---a dynamic, constantly shifting web of manifestations.

**The Bodhi Tree and the Fig Family**

**Bodhi Tree** (*Ficus religiosa*, commonly known as the **Sacred Fig** or **Peepal**) is indeed a true species of fig tree. However, while it produces figs, they are **not the same fruit** as the dried edible figs found in grocery stores.

**Fig Botany: Bodhi Fig vs. Common Edible Fig**

Both trees belong to the genus ***Ficus***, but they are entirely different species:

**(1). The Common Edible Fig (*Ficus carica*)**

**The Commercial Fruit:** The dried or fresh figs bought for food---such as Black Mission, Calimyrna, or Smyrna figs---come strictly from ***Ficus carica***. This species was domesticated thousands of years ago in the Mediterranean and Near East. It produces large, soft, sugar-rich fruits with dense, sweet pulp and fine seeds specifically bred for human eating.

**(2). The Bodhi Tree Fig (*Ficus religiosa*)**

**The Wild/Sacred Fruit:** The fruits of the Bodhi tree are small, round, marble-sized figs (about 1 to 1.5 cm in diameter). When ripe, they turn dark purple or black. They are dry, tough, and filled with tiny seeds, making them unpalatable and virtually inedible for humans. In nature, these small figs serve as an important food source for birds (like green pigeons and barbets), fruit bats, and insects, which digest the pulp and disperse the seeds across the landscape.

**The Unique Nature of Fig \"Fruits\" (*Syconium*)**

In botanical terms, a fig is not actually a single fruit or berry. It is an inverted flower structure called a **syconium**:

- **Inverted Flowers:** The fleshy outer shell is actually a hollow stem tissue lined on the *inside* with hundreds of tiny, individual blossoms.

- **Specialized Pollination:** Fig trees rely on microscopic wasps (*fig wasps*) that enter a tiny opening at the bottom of the fig (the *ostiole*) to pollinate the internal flowers.

- **Universal Structure:** Every single species in the genus *Ficus*---from the giant Banyan (*Ficus benghalensis*) and the Bodhi tree (*Ficus religiosa*) to the garden edible fig (*Ficus carica*)---produces its flowers inside this unique syconium structure.

**Evolutionary Perspective on the Inverted Flower Structure (Syconium)**

From an evolutionary standpoint, the **syconium**---the enclosed, inverted flower structure characteristic of the genus *Ficus*---is **botanically rare and highly specialized**, but evolutionary biology reveals it to be one of the most successful adaptations in the history of plant life. Rather than exposing flowers to the open air like most flowering plants (angiosperms), the fig tree evolved to enclose hundreds of tiny flowers inside a fleshy, hollow vessel.

**(1). Rare Across Plant Families**

Out of more than 400 families of flowering plants, the syconium structure is almost entirely unique to the **Moraceae** (mulberry) family, and specifically perfected within the single genus ***Ficus***. We do not see this enclosed, inside-out flower structure in common fruit trees like apples, berries, citrus, or stone fruits.

**(2). Massively Dominant Within Its Niche**

While structurally rare across the broader plant kingdom, the genus *Ficus* itself contains **over 800 distinct species** spread across tropical and subtropical regions worldwide (including the Bodhi tree, Banyan, Common Fig, and rubber plants). From an evolutionary perspective, this single structural invention allowed *Ficus* to diversify into hundreds of ecological niches, making it one of the largest and most ecologically dominant woody plant genera on Earth.

**Esoteric and Mythological: The Celestial Origin of Ficus**

From an esoteric and mystical perspective, the extraordinary nature of the genus *Ficus*---its enclosed interior world (*syconium*), its 800+ diverse forms, its singular relationship with a hidden messenger (the fig wasp), and its role as a year-round engine of life---frequently leads ancient traditions to view it as a **celestial plant imported or created directly by those ancient gods when they visited earth**.

**(1). The Archetype of the Plant of Divine Seed (*Deva-Vṛkṣa*)**

In Western hermeticism, Vedic esotericism, and ancient Near Eastern myth, highly complex or structurally unique plants were often classified as **celestial imports**---beings brought down from higher realms (*deva-loka* or hyper-dimensional spheres) during primordial eras.

**The Imprinted Macrocosm inside a Microcosm**

- **The Sealed Sacred Chamber:** In hermetic philosophy, the principle \"as above, so below\" requires that physical structures reflect cosmic laws. The *syconium* is a self-contained, subterranean world turned inward---a miniature universe sealed off from external decay.

- **The Divine Geometry:** Mystics view this inverted structure as evidence of intentional divine architecture. While ordinary flora open outward to the raw environment, the *Ficus* creates a protected sanctuary within itself, symbolizing the unmanifest realm (*avyakta*) holding all seeds of life in secret before manifestation.

**(2). Mythological Accounts of Divine Origin**

Several ancient traditions explicitly record the fig tree as a plant brought to Earth by gods or celestial beings:

**A. Vedic Tradition: The Tree Born of Celestial Nectar (*Amṛta*)**

In Vedic esotericism, the *Aśvattha* (and its close kin, the *Nyagrodha* or Banyan) is not considered an ordinary earth plant. Ancient texts record that when the celestial nectar of immortality (*amṛta*) was brought down from the heavens, drops spilled onto the earth, giving rise to sacred trees possessed of extraordinary vitality, hidden fire (*Agni-garbha*), and immense structural variety. It was seen as an earthly projection of the supreme deity\'s cosmic form.

**B. Greco-Roman & Mediterranean Mysteries: Demeter's Gift**

In Hellenic esoteric mysteries, the fig tree was recorded as a special creation given directly to humanity by **Demeter** (or Dionysus). Mythologically, Demeter bestowed the first fig tree upon Phytalios in Attica as a special divine gift, distinct from ordinary terrestrial vegetation, granting both sweet nourishment and deep mystical symbolism related to initiation and secret knowledge hidden beneath a rough exterior.

**C. Mesopotamian and Near Eastern \"Tree of Life\"**

In Sumerian and Babylonian lore, the central Tree of Life (*Mes tree*) planted in the sacred garden by the high gods is frequently depicted with fruit structures reminiscent of the syconium. It served as a cosmic pillar brought down by *Anunnaki* to establish the boundary between heaven and earth and sustain living beings.

**(3). The Mystic Theory of \"Higher Seeding\" & Biological Variety**

The vast variety of over 800 distinct *Ficus* species---each paired with its own hyper-specialized wasp---presents a pattern that esoteric theories attribute to **Intelligent Celestial Seeding**:

1.  **The Cosmic Blueprint (*Mātrikā*):** Esoteric cosmology posits that a single divine archetype (a \"Master Seed\") was introduced into physical earth. As it descended into dense matter, it fractured into hundreds of precise sub-varieties to adapt to every climate, terrain, and continent, ensuring that no ecosystem on Earth would lack its life-sustaining matrix.

2.  **The Angelic/Wasp Messenger:** In mystic symbolism, the tiny fig wasp that navigates the closed dark chamber of the fig is seen as an **initiatory spirit** or subtle messenger (*dūta*). The relationship between tree and wasp is viewed not as a random natural accident, but as a deliberately coded ritual written into biological form---a secret key requiring a specific keyholder to unlock generation.

**[29](javascript:void(0))   anantaś cāsmi nāgānāṁ, varuṇo yādasām aham;**

**       piṭṛṇām aryamā cāsmi, yamaḥ saṁyamatām aham.**

And among nāgas (nāgānām) I am (ca asmi) Ananta (also **Śeṣa-nāga**, the personification of infinitude, timelessness, and absolute permanence.); Varuṇa (the deity of cosmic order, ocean, and primeval waters) among water-beings (yādasām aham); and among ancestors (pitṝṇām) I am (ca asmi) Aryamā (*arya* / *ari*: noble; refers to the Āditya associated with nobility, chivalry, social harmony, and ancestral custom); Yama (the lord of justice and death) among regulators and dispensers of justice (saṁyamatām aham).

**Commentary: *Nāga* vs. *Sarpa***

**sarpa** and **nāga** represent two distinct classifications of reptilian/ serpentine beings, differing in their biology, realm, consciousness, and cosmic function. A **Sarpa** remains a biological reptile crawling on the ground, a **Nāga** is a semi-divine, highly conscious being capable of stepping directly into human society as a person.

**Nāgas can shape-shifting into human form at will, whereas Sarpas are strictly bound to their single physical reptilian form.**

**Nāgas:** Described as *kāma-rūpinaḥ*---beings who can change their shape according to their desire or intent. They frequently transform into human men and women. In human form, they are renowned for extraordinary physical beauty, elegance, and spiritual magnetism. They often wear divine ornaments and retain a subtle mark of their origin---such as a hidden hood, a glowing jewel (*nāga-maṇi*), or a subtle reptilian aura.

**Sarpas:** Entirely lack *kāma-rūpa*. They are physical, biological organisms governed by earthly natural laws. They cannot shed their reptilian form to walk as humans.

Because Nāgas can assume human form and possess high intellect, speech, and culture, they interact extensively with human society in ancient literature:

**Royal Alliances and Lineages:** In the *Mahābhārata*, royal human warriors enter into marriages with Nāgas in human form: **Arjuna and Ulūpī:** Arjuna marries the Nāga princess Ulūpī, who assumes human form when meeting him. **Bhīma and Āryaka:** Bhīma descends to the subterranean realm of the Nāgas (*Pātāla*) and receives strength-giving nectars from his ancestor, the Nāga king Āryaka.

**Guardians of Wisdom and Sacred Teachings:** Because of their proximity to humans, Nāgas often act as intermediaries between divine realms and human seekers, serving as guardians of sacred treasures, hidden scriptures, and esoteric knowledge.

**Earthly *Sarpas*:** These are physical snakes of the mortal world. They creep on their bellies, possess single heads, bite out of self-defense or predatory instinct, and belong entirely to terrestrial ecology. Vāsuki is named as the supreme ruler/exemplar among them because he allowed his body to be used as the rope during *Samudra-manthana* (the churning of the milk ocean), sacrificing himself for the manifestation of the divine nectar (*amṛta*).

**Ananta** (also known as **Śeṣa-nāga**) holds a singular, supreme position in cosmic ontology:

**Ananta (Endless / Boundless):** derived from *an-* (without) + *anta* (end/limit). Ananta is the personification of **infinitude, timelessness, and absolute permanence**.

**The Foundation of Creation (*Anantāsa*):** Ananta is the multi-headed multi-hooded divine serpent upon whose coils Lord Viṣṇu rests in the Causal Ocean (*Kāraṇa-samudra*) during His cosmic sleep (*Yoga-nidrā*). He supports all the universe\'s planetary systems on his expanded hoods.

**Untouched by Cosmic Dissolution (*Śeṣa*):** He is called **Śeṣa** (literally \"that which remains\" or \"the residue\") because when the entire universe undergoes total dissolution (*pralaya*) and all worlds are destroyed, Śeṣa alone remains unaffected. He transcends cosmic cycles of creation and destruction.

**The Principle of Absolute Stability:** In *Vibhūti-Yoga*, while other entities represent functional power, physical strength, or authority within the world, Ananta represents the **unshakeable, infinite foundation** that supports the world from beneath without being attached to it.

**Pāḷi Canonical Stance & Key Interactions**

In the Pāḷi Canon, nāgas are portrayed as intelligent, shape-shifting semi-divine beings who revere Gautama Buddha and seek protection under the Dhamma. However, because their birth is driven by animal-realm karma (*tiracchāna-gati*), they lack the human vessel required to realize complete liberation into the deathless realm of *Nibbāna* in that lifetime.

In the Pāḷi Canon, Gautama Buddha interacts with **nāgas** in numerous discourses (*suttas*) and *Vinaya*, where nāgas are depicted as powerful, semi-divine beings associated with water, subterranean realms, and protection. While they possess extraordinary power and can shape-shift into human form, they are classified as animal-realm beings (*tiracchānayoni*) who cannot attain bhikkhu/bhikkhuni ordination or reach final liberation (*maggaphala*) in that specific birth.

**(1). Mucalinda Nāga Protecting the Buddha (Udāna 2.1)**

After His liberation under the Bodhi tree, a massive storm raged for seven days. The Nāga King **Mucalinda** emerged from his subterranean abode, wrapped his coils around the Buddha seven times, and spread his large hood over the Buddha\'s head to protect Him from cold, wind, and rain.

**Udāna 2.1 (Mucalinda Sutta):** *"Atha kho mucalindo nāgarājā sakabhavanā nikkhamma bhagavato kāyaṁ sattakkhattuṁ bhogehi parikkhipitvā upari mūrdhani mahantaṁ phaṇaṁ karitvā aṭṭhāsi."*

\"Then Mucalinda, king of the nāgas, leaving his own abode, encircled the body of the Blessed One (*bhagava*) seven times with his coils and stood with his great hood expanded over the Blessed One\'s head\...\"

When the storm cleared, Mucalinda unfolded himself, transformed into a young Brahmana man, and paid homage to Gautama Buddha.

**(2). Taming the Fierce Fire-Nāga at Uruvelā (Vinaya Mahāvagga, Khaṇḍhaka 1)**

To teach the three Kassapa brothers (fire-worshipping matted-hair ascetics), Gautama Buddha spent the night in their sacred fire-hut, which was inhabited by a dangerous, venomous fire-breathing nāga.

**Vinaya Piṭaka, Mahāvagga (Uruvelā-pāṭihāriya):** *"Atha kho so nāgo bhagavantaṁ pravissantaṁ disvā dukkhī dummano kupito dhūmāyasi... Bhagavāpi tejena tejaṁ pariyādiyitvā dhūmāyasi..."*

\"Then that nāga, seeing the Blessed One enter, was distressed, displeased, and angry, and emitted smoke... The Blessed One, subduing the nāga\'s fire with His own spiritual power, also emitted smoke...\"

The Buddha subdued the nāga\'s intense heat and venom using His supernormal spiritual power (*iddhipāṭihāriya*), placing the defeated nāga harmlessly inside His alms bowl to show the Kassapa brothers.

**(3). The Ordination Attempt (Vinaya Mahāvagga, Khaṇḍhaka 1)**

A nāga, tired of his serpentine rebirth, shape-shifted into a human man and took full bhikkhu ordination (*upasampadā*). However, while sleeping deeply in his cell, his shape-shifting power failed, and his true body---filling the entire hut with serpent coils---was discovered by another bhikkhu.

The Buddha called the nāga and explained why a nāga cannot remain in the Saṅgha: **Vinaya Piṭaka, Mahāvagga (Nāgavatthu):** *"Anabhiññātā eke nāgā... Avirūḷhiṁ pāpuṇanti imasmiṁ dhammavinaye. Nāttheyyaṁ, nāga, imasmiṁ dhammavinaye upasampadā. Gaccha tvaṁ, nāga, tadeva uposathaṁ upavasa."*

\"Nāgas are incapable of growth in this Dhamma and Vinaya. There is no ordination for you, nāga, in this Dhamma and Vinaya. Go, nāga, and observe the Uposatha (observance days) on the fourteenth and fifteenth days\...\"

The nāga wept bitterly upon hearing this. To honor the nāga\'s devotion, the Buddha decreed that all candidates seeking bhikkhu ordination must first be asked the formal question: "Are you a human being?"---a screening question still asked in Theravāda bhikkhu ordination ceremonies today.

**(4). Arahant Nanda Taming the Nāga Kings (Māyā / Ahina Sutta & Canon Commentaries)**

In addition to Gautam the Supreme Arahant Himself, a senior Arahant disciples like **Venerable Mahāmoggallāna** tamed fierce cosmic nāgas (such as **Nandopananda**) on Gautama Buddha\'s instruction using higher spiritual powers.

Furthermore, in the **Ahi Sutta (AN 4.67 / Vinaya Cūḷavagga)**, Gautama Buddha instructed the bhikkhus to radiate loving-kindness (*mettā*) toward all four royal families of nāgas (*virūpakkha*, *erāpatha*, *chabyāputta*, *kaṇhāgotamaka*) (the **four royal nāga families** (*cattāri rājakulāni nāgānaṁ*) as the supreme ruling lineages over the entire serpentine world on earth) to prevent snakebites: **Anguttara Nikāya 4.67 (Ahi Sutta):** *"Virūpakkhihi me mettaṁ, mettaṁ erāpathehi me;* *Chabyāputtehi me mettaṁ, mettaṁ kaṇhāgotamakehi ca."*

\"My loving-kindness is with the Virūpakkhas, my loving-kindness with the Erāpathas; my loving-kindness with the Chabyāputtas, and my loving-kindness with the Kaṇhāgotamakas.\"

Among purifiers (pavatām), I am (asmi) the wind (pavanaḥ); among wielders of weapons (śastra-bhṛtām), I am (aham) Rāma (the archetype of righteousness and supreme martial prowess); among aquatic creatures / fish (jhaṣāṇām) too (ca), I am (asmi) the sea-monster (traditionally signifies a mythical sea-monster, crocodile, or some aquatic apex creature) (makaraḥ); among flowing streams / rivers (srotasām), I am (asmi) the Ganges (jāhnavī).

**Commentary**: **The Four Major Elemental Purifiers**

**The wind (*pavana*) is considered one of the primary purifiers in the Indian tradition**, though whether it is the single \"best\" depends on the philosophical or scriptural context, as **fire (*agni*)**, **water (*āpas* / *gaṅgā*)**, and **the breath (*prāṇa*)** are also revered as supreme purifying agents. In the Indian tradition, the concept of purification (*pavana* / *śodhana*) operates on multiple levels---physical, elemental, ritual, and spiritual:

In classical Indian cosmology (*Pañcamahābhūta*), four main elements act as natural purifiers:

1.  **Wind (*Vāyu* / *Pavana*):** Cleanses through perpetual motion, aeration, and sweeping away physical and atmospheric stagnation.

2.  **Fire (*Agni*):** Cleanses by consuming, burning away impurities (*dosa*), and transforming matter into pure ash. It is often described as *pāvaka* (the consumer-purifier).

3.  **Water (*Āpas* / *Gaṅgā*):** Cleanses by washing away physical and symbolic dirt, carrying away impurities through flow.

4.  **Sunlight (*Sūrya*):** Cleanses by illuminating, drying, and dispelling darkness/pathogens.

**Wind as *Prāṇa* (Life-Breath)**

In yoga *Prāṇāyāma* and the Upaniṣads, wind manifests inside the physical body as **Prāṇa** (vital breath): The Upaniṣads describe *Prāṇa* as the one internal deity that **cannot be corrupted by evil or sensory desire** because it is constantly moving and purifying. By practicing breath control (*prāṇāyāma*), the inner \"wind\" purifies the subtle channels (*nāḍīs*) and stills mental agitation.

**How Wind Compares to Other \"Supreme\" Purifiers**

**For Physical & Atmospheric Purity:** **Wind (*Pavana*)** is supreme because it moves everywhere without attachment, dispersing stagnant decay.

**For Ritual & Elemental Purity:** **Fire (*Agni*)** is often highlighted because it cannot be contaminated---it incinerates everything dirty and leaves only pure essence.

**For Spiritual & Karmic Purity:** Sacred waters, particularly the **Ganges (*Gaṅgā*)**, are cited as the supreme physical water-purifier that washes away negative karmic impressions.

Of those who subdue / tame / restrain (damayatām), I am (asmi) the rod of discipline / punishment (daṇḍaḥ); of those who seek victory (jigīṣatām), I am (asmi) statesmanship / wise strategy (nītiḥ); of mysteries (guhyānām), I am (asmi) indeed (eva) and (ca) silence /inward stillness (maunam); \[and\] of the wise / possessors of knowledge (jñānavatām), I am (aham) knowledge (jñānam).

**[39](javascript:void(0))   yac cāpi sarva-bhūtānāṁ, bījaṁ tad aham arjuna;**

**       na tad asti vinā yat syān, mayā bhūtaṁ carācaram.**

And (ca) whatever (yat) is also (api) the seed (bījam) of all beings (sarva-bhūtānām), that (tat) I am (aham), O Arjuna (arjuna); there is (asti) no (na) manifestations (bhūtam), moving or unmoving (carācaram), that (tat) could exist (syāt) without (vinā) Me (mayā).

**[40](javascript:void(0))   nānto 'sti mama divyānāṁ, vibhūtīnāṁ parantapa;**

**       eṣa tūddeśataḥ prokto, vibhūter vistaro mayā.**

There is (asti) no (na) limit (antaḥ) to My (mama) divine (divyānām) manifestations (vibhūtīnām), O scorcher of foes (parantapa); but (tu) this (eṣaḥ) detailed description (vistaraḥ) of \[My\] manifestation (vibhūteḥ) has been declared (proktaḥ) by Me (mayā) merely by a summary (uddeśataḥ).

**[41](javascript:void(0))   yad yad vibhūtimat sattvaṁ, śrīmad ūrjitam eva vā;**

**       tat tad evāvagaccha tvaṁ, mama tejo-'ṁśa-sambhavam.**

Whatever (yad yad) existence (sattvam) possesses manifestation power (vibhūtimat), or (vā) is endowed with radiance (śrīmat), \[or\] indeed (eva) mighty / powerful (ūrjitam); that very (tat tad eva) \[being\] know (avagaccha) you (tvam) to be arisen from (sambhavam) a fraction (aṁśa) of My (mama) splendor / radiant power (tejaḥ).

**[42](javascript:void(0))   atha vā bahunaitena, kiṁ jñātena tavārjuna;**

**       viṣṭabhyāham idaṁ kṛtsnam, ekāṁśena sthito jagat.**

Or (atha vā), what (kim) is the need for this (etena) vast (bahunā) knowledge (jñātena) to be acquired by you (tava), O Arjuna (arjuna)? I (aham) support and sustain (viṣṭabhya) this (idam) entire (kṛtsnam) human world (jagat), remain established (sthitaḥ) by a single fraction / single portion (ekāṁśena).

### Chapter 11 Viśva-Rūpa Darśana Yoga: The Yoga of Seeing the Cosmic Form

***Arjuna uvāca***

**[1](javascript:void(0))   mad-anugrahāya paramaṁ, guhyam adhyātma-saṁjñitam;**

**     yat tvayoktaṁ vacas tena, moho 'yaṁ vigato mama.**

Arjuna said: my highest favor (*mad-anugrahāya*), the supreme (*paramaṁ*) mystery (*guhyam*) known as the high *ātmā* (*adhyātma-saṁjñitam*) which (*yat*) words (*vacaḥ*) has been spoken (*uktam*) by You (*tvayā*)---by that (*tena*), this (*ayam*) ignorance (*mohaḥ*) of mine (*mama*) is completely removed (*vigataḥ*).

**[2](javascript:void(0))   bhavāpyayau hi bhūtānāṁ, śrutau vistaraśo mayā;**

**     tvattaḥ kamala-patrākṣa, māhātmyam api cāvyayam.**

For (*hi*) the origin and dissolution (*bhavāpyayau*) of beings (*bhūtānāṁ*) have been heard (*śrutau*) in full detail (*vistaraśaḥ*) by me (*mayā*) from You (*tvattaḥ*), O lotus-petal-eyed One (*kamala-patra-akṣa*), as well as (*api ca*) Your imperishable (*avyayam*) great *ātmā* (*māhātmyam*).

**[3](javascript:void(0))   evam etad yathāttha tvam, ātmānaṁ parameśvara;**

**     draṣṭum icchāmi te rūpam, aiśvaraṁ puruṣottama.**

Just so it is (*evam etat*) as (*yathā*) You (*tvam*) have declared (*āttha*) the *ātmā,* O Supreme Lord (*parama-īśvara*). I wish (*icchāmi*) to see (*draṣṭum*) Your (*te*) lordly (*aiśvaram*) form (*rūpam*), O Unsurpassed Person (*puruṣa-uttama*).

**[4](javascript:void(0))   manyase yadi tac chakyaṁ, mayā draṣṭum iti prabho;**

**     yogeśvara tato me tvaṁ, darśayātmānam avyayam.**

If (*yadi*) You think (*manyase*) that (*tat*) it is possible (*śakyam*) to be seen (*draṣṭum*) by me (*mayā*), O Master (*prabho*), then (*tatas*), O Lord of Yoga (*yoga-īśvara*), do You (*tvam*) show (*darśaya*) Your (*me*) imperishable *ātmā* (*ātmānam avyayam*).

***Śrī-Bhagavān uvāca***

**[5](javascript:void(0))   paśya me pārtha rūpāṇi, śataśo 'tha sahasraśaḥ;**

**     nānā-vidhāni divyāni, nānā-varṇākṛtīni ca.**

Bhagava Kṛṣṇa said: Look (*paśya*), O son of Pṛthā (*pārtha*), My (*me*) forms (*rūpāṇi*) by hundreds (*śataśaḥ*) and then (*atha*) by thousands (*sahasraśaḥ*)---manifold, of various kinds (*nānā-vidhāni*), divine (*divyāni*), and (*ca*) of diverse colors and shapes (*nānā-varṇa-ākṛtīni*).

**[6](javascript:void(0))   paśyādityān vasūn rudrān, aśvinau marutas tathā;**

**     bahūny adṛṣṭa-pūrvāṇi, paśyāścaryāṇi bhārata.**

Look (*paśya*) the Ādityas (*ādityān*), the Vasus (*vasūn*), the Rudras (*rudrān*), the two Aśvins (*aśvinau*), as well as (*tathā*) the Maruts (*marutaḥ*); Look (*paśya*), O descendant of Bharata (*bhārata*), many (*bahūni*) marvelous sights (*āścaryāṇi*) unseen before (*adṛṣṭa-pūrvāṇi*).

**[7](javascript:void(0))   ihaika-sthaṁ jagat kṛtsnaṁ, paśyādya sa-carācaram;**

**     mama dehe guḍākeśa, yac cānyad draṣṭum icchasi.**

Look (*paśya*) today (*adya*) the entire (*kṛtsnam*) humanity / human lineage (*jagat*), moving and unmoving (*sa-cara-acaram*), centered standing in one place (*eha-ekastham*) right here (*iha*) in My (*mama*) body (*dehe*), O conqueror of sleep (*guḍākeśa*), as well as (*ca*) whatever else (*yat anyat*) you wish (*icchasi*) to see (*draṣṭum*).

**[8](javascript:void(0))   na tu māṁ śakyase draṣṭum, anenaiva sva-cakṣuṣā;**

**     divyaṁ dadāmi te cakṣuḥ, paśya me yogam aiśvaram.**

But (*tu*) you are not (*na*) able (*śakyase*) to see (*draṣṭum*) Me (*mām*) with these (*anena*) own eyes (*sva-cakṣuṣā*) alone (*eva*); I give (*dadāmi*) to you (*te*) divine vision (*divyaṁ cakṣuḥ*)---See (*paśya*) My (*me*) lordly yoga / union (*yogam aiśvaram*).

***\***

***Sañjaya uvāca***

**[9](javascript:void(0))   evam uktvā tato rājan, mahā-yogeśvaro hariḥ;**

**     darśayām āsa pārthāya, paramaṁ rūpam aiśvaram.**

Sañjaya said: Having thus spoken (*evam uktvā*), then (*tatas*), O King (*rājan*), Hari (*hariḥ*), the Great Lord of Yoga (*mahā-yoga-īśvaraḥ*), showed (*darśayām āsa*) to the son of Pṛthā (*pārthāya*) His supreme (*paramam*) lordly form (*rūpam aiśvaram*).

**[10](javascript:void(0))   aneka-vaktra-nayanam, anekādbhuta-darśanam;**

**       aneka-divyābharaṇaṁ, divyānekodyatāyudham.**

\[Arjuna saw that supreme form,\] having many mouths and eyes (*aneka-vaktra-nayanam*), presenting many wondrous sights (*aneka-adbhuta-darśanam*), adorned with many divine ornaments (*aneka-divya-ābharaṇam*), and brandishing many uplifted divine weapons (*divya-aneka-udyata-āyudham*).

**[11](javascript:void(0))   divya-mālyāmbara-dharaṁ, divya-gandhānulepanam;**

**       sarvāścarya-mayaṁ devam, anantaṁ viśvato-mukham.**

\[Arjuna saw that supreme form,\] wearing divine garlands and garments (*divya-mālya-ambara-dharam*), anointed with divine fragrances and unguents (*divya-gandha-anulepanam*), full of all wonders (*sarva-āścarya-mayam*), *deva*, infinite (*anantam*), with faces facing every direction (*viśvataḥ-mukham*).

**[12](javascript:void(0))   divi sūrya-sahasrasya, bhaved yugapad utthitā;**

**       yadi bhāḥ sadṛśī sā syād, bhāsas tasya mahātmanaḥ.**

If the radiance (*bhāḥ*) of a thousand suns (*sūrya-sahasrasya*) were to burst forth (*utthitā bhavet*) simultaneously (*yugapad*) in the sky (*divi*), that brilliance might resemble (*sā sadṛśī syāt*) the radiance (*bhāsas*) of that great *ātmā* (*tasya mahātmanaḥ*).

**[13](javascript:void(0))   tatraika-stham jagat kṛtsnaṁ, pravibhaktam anekadhā;**

**       apaśyad deva-devasya, śarīre pāṇḍavas tadā.**

There (*tatra*), abiding in one (*eka-stham*), O son of Pāṇḍu (Arjuna) (*pāṇḍavaḥ*), then (*tadā*) Look! (*apaśyat*) the entire humanity (*jagat kṛtsnam*), though divided (*pravibhaktam*) into countless divisions (*anekadhā*), within the form (*śarīre*) is the Deva of devas (*deva-devasya*).

**[14](javascript:void(0))   tataḥ sa vismayāviṣto, hṛṣṭa-romā dhanañjayaḥ;**

**       praṇamya śirasā devaṁ, kṛtāñjalir abhāṣata.**

Then (*tataḥ*), he (*saḥ*), O Conqueror of Wealth (Arjuna) (*dhanañjayaḥ*), overwhelmed with wonder (*vismaya-āviṣṭaḥ*), his hair standing on end (*hṛṣṭa-romā*), bowing down (*praṇamya*) with his head (*śirasā*) to the *Deva*, with folded hands (*kṛta-añjaliḥ*), spoke (*abhāṣata*).

***Arjuna uvāca***

**[15](javascript:void(0))   paśyāmi devāṁs tava deva dehe, sarvāṁs tathā bhūta-viśeṣa-saṇghān; brahmāṇam īśaṁ kamalāsana-stham, ṛṣīṁś ca sarvān uragāṁś ca divyān.**

Arjuna said: I see (*paśyāmi*) the *devas* in Your body (*tava dehe*), O D*eva*, and similarly (*tathā*) all (*sarvān*) the various assembled beings (*bhūta-viśeṣa-saṅghān*); Lord Brahmā (*brahmāṇam īśam*) seated on his lotus seat (*kamala-āsana-stham*), and all (*sarvān*) the sages/ seers (*ṛṣīn*) and divine serpents (*uragān divyān*).

**[16](javascript:void(0))   aneka-bāhūdara-vaktra-netraṁ, paśyāmi tvā sarvato 'nanta-rūpam; nāntaṁ na madhyaṁ na punas tavādiṁ, paśyāmi viśveśvara viśva-rūpa.**

I see You (*paśyāmi tvā*) having countless arms, bellies, mouths, and eyes (*aneka-bāhu-udara-vaktra-netram*), manifesting everywhere (*sarvataḥ*) in infinite forms (*ananta-rūpam*). O Lord of All (*viśva-īśvara*), All Form (*viśva-rūpa*), I see (*paśyāmi*) neither Your end (*na antam*), nor Your middle (*na madhyam*), nor again Your beginning (*na punaḥ tava ādim*).

**[17](javascript:void(0))   kirīṭinaṁ gadinaṁ cakriṇaṁ ca, tejo-rāśiṁ sarvato dīptimantam; paśyāmi tvāṁ durnirīkṣyaṁ samantād, dīptānalārka-dyutim aprameyam.**

I see You (*paśyāmi tvām*) wearing a crown (*kirīṭinam*), bearing a mace (*gadinam*) and a discus (*cakriṇam ca*); a mass of radiance (*tejo-rāśim*), glowing radiant on all sides (*sarvataḥ dīptimantam*), hard to gaze upon (*durnirīkṣyam*) from every direction (*samantāt*), blazing with the brilliance of burning fire and the sun (*dīpta-anala-arka-dyutim*), boundless (*aprameyam*).

**[18](javascript:void(0))   tvam akṣaraṁ paramaṁ veditavyaṁ, tvam asya viśvasya paraṁ nidhānam; tvam avyayaḥ śāśvata-dharma-goptā, sanātanas tvaṁ puruṣo mato me.**

For You (tvam) \[are\] the supreme (paramam) imperishable (akṣaram) to be known (veditavyam); You (tvam) \[are\] the supreme (param) foundation (nidhānam) of this (asya) whole manifestation (viśvasya). You (tvam) \[are\] imperishable (avyayaḥ), the protector and guardian (goptā) of the eternal dharma (śāśvata-dharma); and in my (me) realization (mataḥ), You (tvam) \[are\] the primeval (sanātanaḥ) person (puruṣaḥ).

**[19](javascript:void(0))   anādi-madyāntam ananta-vīryam, ananta-bāhuṁ śaśi-sūrya-netram; paśyāmi tvāṁ dīpta-hutāśa-vaktraṁ, sva-tejasā viśvam idaṁ tapantam.**

I see You (paśyāmi tvām) as without beginning, middle, or end (anādi-madhya-antam), of boundless energy (ananta-vīryam), with countless arms (ananta-bāhum), having the moon and sun as Your eyes (śaśi-sūrya-netram). I see You (paśyāmi tvām) with a mouth blazing like sacrificial fire (dīpta-hutāśa-vaktram), illuminating (tapantam) this (idam) all manifestation (viśvam) by Your own radiance (sva-tejasā).

**[20](javascript:void(0))   dyāv ā-pṛthivyor idam antaraṁ hi, vyāptaṁ tvayaikena diśaś ca sarvāḥ; dṛṣṭvābhutaṁ rūpam idaṁ tavograṁ, loka-trayaṁ pravyathitaṁ mahātman.**

For (*hi*) this (*idam*) space between (*antaraṃ*) heaven (*dyāv*) and earth (*pṛthivyoḥ*), as well as (*ca*) all (*sarvāḥ*) directions (*diśaḥ*), is pervaded (*vyāptaṃ*) by you (*tvayā*) alone (*ekena*); having seen (*dṛṣṭvā*) this (*idam*) wonderful (*adbhutaṃ*), forceful (*ugraṃ*) form (*rūpaṃ*) of yours (*tava*), the three worlds (*loka-trayaṃ*) are trembling (*pravyathitaṃ*), O Great *Ātmā* (*mahātman*).

**[21](javascript:void(0))   amī hi tvā sura-saṇghā viśanti, kecid bhītāḥ prāñjalayo gṛṇanti; svastīty uktvā maharṣi-siddha-saṇghāḥ, stuvanti tvāṁ stutibhiḥ puṣkalābhiḥ.**

For (*hi*) these (*amī*) groups of asuras (*sura-saṅghāḥ*) enter into (*viśanti*) you (*tvā*); some (*kecid*), terrified (*bhītāḥ*), offer praise (*gṛṇanti*) with joined palms (*prāñjalayaḥ*); declaring (*uktvā*) \"May it be well.\" (*svasti iti*), groups of great seers and perfected ones (*maharṣi-siddha-saṅghāḥ*) praise (*stuvanti*) you (*tvāṃ*) with abundant, sublime hymns (*stutibhiḥ puṣkalābhiḥ*).

**[22](javascript:void(0))   rudrādityā vasavo ye ca sādhyā, viśve 'svinau marutaś coṣmapāś ca; gandharva-yakṣāsura-siddha-saṇghā, vīkṣante tvaṁ vismitāś caiva sarve.**

The Rudras (*rudrāḥ*), Ādityas (*ādityāḥ*), Vasus (*vasavaḥ*), and (*ca*) those (*ye*) who are Sādhyas (*sādhyāḥ*); the Viśvedevas (*viśve*), the two Aśvins (*aśvinau*), the Maruts (*marutaḥ*), and (*ca*) the Ancestors (*ūṣmapāḥ*), as well as (*ca*); hosts of Gandharvas, Yakṣas, Asuras, and Siddhas (*gandharva-yakṣāsura-siddha-saṅghāḥ*)---all (*sarve*) of them indeed (*ca eva*) gaze upon (*vīkṣante*) you (*tvām*), struck with wonder and awe (*vismitāḥ*).

**[23](javascript:void(0))   rūpaṁ mahat te bahu-vaktra-netraṁ, mahā-bāho bahu-bāhūru-pādam; bahūdaraṁ bahu-daṁṣṭrā-karālaṁ, dṛṣṭvā lokāḥ pravyathitās tathāham.**

Having seen (*dṛṣṭvā*) your (*te*) immense (*mahat*) form (*rūpaṃ*), O mighty-armed one (*mahā-bāho*), with many mouths and eyes (*bahu-vaktra-netraṃ*), with many arms, thighs, and feet (*bahu-bāhūru-pādam*), with many bellies (*bahūdaraṃ*), terrifying with many tusks / fangs (*bahu-daṁṣṭrā-karālaṃ*), the worlds (*lokāḥ*) are trembling (*pravyathitāḥ*), and so am I (*tathā aham*).

**[24](javascript:void(0))   nabhaḥ-spṛśaṁ dīptam aneka-varṇaṁ, vyāttānanaṁ dīpta-viśāla-netram; dṛṣṭvā hi tvāṁ pravyathitāntar-ātmā, dhṛtiṁ na vindāmi śamaṁ ca viṣṇo.**

Having seen (dṛṣṭvā hi) You (tvām) touching the sky / reaching the heavens (nabhaḥ-spṛśam), radiant and blazing (dīptam), displaying multiple forms and colors (aneka-varṇam), with mouth wide gaping open (vyāttānanam), and possessing fiery, immense eyes (dīpta-viśāla-netram), my inner ātmā (antar-ātmā) is trembling (pravyathita); I do not find (na vindāmi) composure (dhṛtim) nor calmness (śamam ca), O All-Pervading One (viṣṇo).

**[25](javascript:void(0))   daṁṣṭrā-karālāni ca te mukhāni, dṛṣṭvaiva kālānala-sannibhāni; diśo na jāne na labhe ca śarma, prasīda deveśa jagan-nivāsa.**

Having seen (dṛṣṭvā eva) Your (te) mouths (mukhāni) with dreadful and terrifying protruding fangs/teeth. (daṁṣṭrā-karālāni), which resemble the fires of cosmic destruction at the end of time (kālānala-sannibhāni), I lose orientation (diśaḥ na jāne) and I do not obtain (na labhe ca) safety or refuge (śarma); be gracious and merciful (prasīda), O Lord of the devas (deveśa), O Abode for the Humanity (jagan-nivāsa).

**[26](javascript:void(0))   amī ca tvāṁ dhṛtarāṣṭrasya putrāḥ, sarve sahaivāvani-pāla-saṇghaiḥ; bhīṣmo droṇaḥ sūta-putras tathāsau, sahāsmadīyair api yodha-mukhyaiḥ.**

All these (amī ca) sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra (dhṛtarāṣṭrasya putrāḥ), together with (sarve sahā eva) the groups of king-protectors of the earth (avani-pāla-saṅghaiḥ); Bhīṣma (bhīṣmaḥ), Droṇa (droṇaḥ), and that son of a charioteer, Karṇa (sūta-putraḥ tathā asau), together with (saha) the chief heroic warriors (yodha-mukhyaiḥ) of our side as well (asmadīyaiḥ api), \[are rapidly rushing into\] You (tvām).

**[27](javascript:void(0))   vaktrāṇi te tvaramāṇā viśanti, daṁṣṭrā-karālāni bhayānakāni; kecid vilagnā daśanāntareṣu, sandṛśyante cūrṇitair uttamāṇgaiḥ.**

Hurrying (tvaramāṇāḥ), they are entering (viśanti) into Your (te) gaping mouths (vaktrāṇi), which are fearful with terrible fangs and tusks (daṁṣṭrā-karālāni) and inspiring utter terror (bhayānakāni); some among them (kecid), trapped and stuck (vilagnāḥ) in the gaps between the teeth (daśanāntareṣu), are seen and observed (sandṛśyante) with their heads (uttamāṅgaiḥ) crushed and pulverized to powder (cūrṇitaiḥ).

**[28](javascript:void(0))   yathā nadīnāṁ bahavo 'mbu-vegāḥ, samudram evābhimukhā dravanti; tathā tavāmī nara-loka-vīrā, viśanti vaktrāṇy abhivijvalanti.**

Just as (yathā) the many (bahavaḥ) rushing torrents of water (ambu-vegāḥ) of the rivers (nadīnām) flow rapidly and run (dravanti) facing directly toward (abhimukhāḥ) the ocean alone (samudram eva); so indeed (tathā) these (amī) heroes/vīrā of the world of men (nara-loka-vīrāḥ) enter (viśanti) Your (tava) blazingly flaming (abhivijvalanti) mouths (vaktrāṇi).

**[29](javascript:void(0))   yathā pradīptaṁ jvalanaṁ pataṇgā, viśanti nāśāya samṛddha-vegāḥ; tathaiva nāśāya viśanti lokās, tavāpi vaktrāṇi samṛddha-vegāḥ.**

Just as (yathā) moths and flying insects (pataṅgāḥ) with accelerated, full momentum (samṛddha-vegāḥ) enter and rush (viśanti) into a blazingly kindled fire (pradīptaṁ jvalanam) for their own destruction and demise (nāśāya); even so indeed (tathā eva), all worlds (lokāḥ) enter (viśanti) Your (tava) blazingly burning mouths (vaktrāṇi api) with full, swift momentum (samṛddha-vegāḥ) for their own destruction (nāśāya).

**[30](javascript:void(0))   lelihyase grasamānaḥ samantāl, lokān samagrān vadanair jvaladbhiḥ; tejobhir āpūrya jagat samagraṁ, bhāsas tavogrāḥ pratapanti viṣṇo.**

Licking up repeatedly and devouring (lelihyase) while swallowing and consuming (grasamānaḥ) from all sides (samantāt) all (samagrān) worlds (lokān) with Your blazing, flaming mouths (vadanaiḥ jvaladbhiḥ); completely pervading (āpūrya) the entire (samagram) humanity (jagat) with fire (tejobhiḥ), Your (tava) terrifying rays / light beams (bhāsaḥ ugrāḥ) burn and scorch intense heat (pratapanti), O aAll-Pervading One! (viṣṇo).

**[31](javascript:void(0))   ākhyāhi me ko bhavān ugra-rūpo, namo 'stu te deva-vara prasīda; vijñātum icchāmi bhavantam ādyaṁ, na hi prajānāmi tava pravṛttim.**

Tell me (ākhyāhi me) who is this manifest (kaḥ bhavān) possessing such a fierce, terrifying form (ugra-rūpaḥ); let homage and salutations be (namaḥ astu) unto You (te), O Foremost of the devas (deva-vara); be gracious and merciful (prasīda); I wish to know and comprehend (vijñātum icchāmi) You, the Primal and Original One (bhavantam ādyam), for indeed I do not comprehend or understand (na hi prajānāmi) Your active intent and cosmic manifestation (tava pravṛttim).

***\***

***Śrī-Bhagavān uvāca***

**[32](javascript:void(0))   kālo 'smi loka-kṣaya-kṛt pravṛddho, lokān samāhartum iha pravṛttaḥ; ṛte 'pi tvāṁ na bhaviṣyanti sarve, ye 'vasthitāḥ pratyanīkeṣu yodhāḥ.**

I am (asmi) Time (kāla), the mighty (pravṛddha ) world-destroyer (lokakṣayakṛt), set in motion (pravṛtta) here (iha) to destroy (samāhartum) the worlds (lokān). Do not (mā) be distressed (vyathiṣṭhāḥ)! Fight (yudhyasva)! You shall conquer (jetāsi) the rivals (sapatnān) in battle (raṇe)!

And (ca) why (kasmāt) should they not (na) bow down (nameran) to You (te), O Great *Ātmā* (mahātman), who are more venerable (garīyase) even (api) than Brahma (brahmaṇaḥ), the primal creator (ādi-kartre). O Infinite One (ananta), Lord of the devas (deveśa), abode of the humanity (jagan-nivāsa), You (tvam) are the imperishable (akṣaraṁ), the existent (sat) and the non-existent (asat), and that which (yat) is beyond (paraṁ) them both!

You (tvam) are the primal deva (ādi-devaḥ), the ancient (purāṇas) Person (puruṣaḥ); You (tvam) are the supreme (param) foundation (nidhānam) of this (asya) cosmic all (viśvasya). You are the knower (vettā), You are to be known (vedyam), and \[You are\] (ca) the supreme (param) dhāma; by You (tvayā) the entire universe (viśvam) is pervaded (tatam), O the Infinite Form (ananta-rūpa).

You (tvam) are the wind god (vāyuḥ), Yama, God of Death and Justice (yamaḥ), the fire god (agniḥ), Varuṇa, God of Waters (varuṇaḥ), the moon (śaśāṅkaḥ), the **Father of Progeny** (prajāpatiḥ), and (ca) the Great **Father of Progeny** (prapitāmahaḥ). May there be (astu) obeisance (namaḥ), obeisance (namaḥ) unto You (te) a thousand times (sahasra-kṛtvaḥ); and (ca) again (punar), and yet again (bhūyaḥ api), obeisance (namaḥ), obeisance (namaḥ) unto You (te).

**Commentary**: Moon God, the **Father of Progeny**

In the Veda, **pati** grammatically denotes "father", \"lord\" or \"master,\" \"to protect, guard, or nourish.\" **Prajāpati** is the **Father of Progeny**.

**Prajāpati as the Primordial Father**

Throughout the *Vedas* and *Brāhmaṇas*, Prajāpati\'s identity as the Father is explicitly declared:

**Rigveda (10.121.10 - Hiraṇyagarbha Sūkta):** *prajāpate na tvad etāny anyo viśvā jātāni pari tā babhūva \"O Prajāpati! None other than You encompasses all these created beings.\"*

**Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa (1.6.3.5 / 2.2.4.11):** *prajāpatir vai pitā / prajāpatir janayitā \"Prajāpati is indeed the Father; Prajāpati is the Begetter/ Generator.\"*

**(1). The Moon as Prajāpati (The Father of Earthly Beings)**

In the Vedic and Upaniṣadic cosmology, Moon is explicitly declared to be **Prajāpati**---the paternal source from which all physical creatures (*prajā*) are produced on Earth.

**Praśna Upaniṣad 1.4--1.5:** *sa prajāpati-kāmah prajā asṛjata \| sa tapo \'tapyata sa tapas taptvā sa mithunam utpādayate \| rayiṃ ca prāṇaṃ cādityo vai prāṇo rayir eva candramā\...*

\"Prajāpati (the Creator/Father) desired progeny (*prajā*). He engaged in primordial force (*tapas*), and having exerted primordial force, he brought forth a pair: Matter/Form (*rayi*) and Life-Energy (*prāṇa*). The Sun is indeed *prāṇa*, and **the Moon (*candramā*) is indeed *rayi* (Matter/Form)**\...\"

**Praśna Upaniṣad 1.9 & 1.12:** *māso vai prajāpatiḥ\... annaṃ vai prajāpatiḥ tato ha vai tad-reto jāyate tasmād imāḥ prajāḥ prajāyante iti \|\|*

\"The Month (governed by the lunar cycle) is indeed Prajāpati\... Food is indeed Prajāpati; from that food is produced the generative seed (*retas*), and from that seed, these progeny (*prajā*) are born into this world.\"

**(2). The Moon as Soul Collector and the Gate of Reincarnation**

The Upaniṣads explicitly define the Moon as the destination for all souls who depart the physical body without unconditioned transcendence. It acts as a collection vessel, testing the knowledge of the departed souls before returning them to Earth.

**Kaushītaki Upaniṣad 1.2 (The Lunar Gate and Collector of Souls):** *ye vai ke cāsmāl lokāt prayanti candramasam eva te sarve gacchanti\... etad vai svargasya lokasya dvāraṃ yac candramāḥ \| taṃ yat pratyāha tam iha vṛṣṭir bhūtvā varṣati sa iha kīṭo vā pataṅgo vā matsyo vā śakunir vā simho vā varāho vā paraśvānvā puruṣo vā\...*

\"**All who depart from this world go directly to the Moon (*candramasa*).** \[\...\] The Moon is indeed the gateway to the heavenly/upper world. Those who cannot answer its query are turned into rain and fall down to Earth; they are reborn here according to their deeds (*kamma*) as a worm, an insect, a fish, a bird, a lion, a boar, or a human being\...\"

**Chāndogya Upaniṣad 5.10.3--5.10.5 (Pitṛyāna: The Ancestral Route):** *atha ya ime grāma iṣṭāpūrte dattam ity upāsate te dhūmam abhisambhavanti\... māsaiḥ pitṛlokam pitṛlokād ākāśam ākāśāc candramasam eṣa somo rājā tad devānām annaṃ taṃ devā bhakṣayanti*

\"Those who perform worldly rituals and good deeds pass into smoke, from smoke into night, from night into the dark fortnight, and from the dark fortnight into the six months of the southern path of the sun\... From those months they go to the realm of the ancestors (*pitṛ-loka*), from the realm of the ancestors to space, and **from space to the Moon. This Moon is King Soma; it is the food of the gods, and the gods consume it.**\" \[Like in the movie "Jupiter.Ascending.2015", the gods harvest souls and consume the souls.\]

**(3). The Reincarnation Mechanism: From Moon back to Earthly Birth**

The *Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad* outlines the precise descent sequence from the lunar holding node back into physical embodiment on Earth.

**Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 6.2.16:** *atha ye yajñena dānena tapasā lokañ jayanti te dhūmam abhisambhavanti\... māsaiḥ pitṛlokam, pitṛlokāc candram, te candram prāpyānnam bhavanti, tāṃs tatra devā yathā somaṃ rājānam āpyāyasvāpakṣīyasvetv evam enāṃs tatra bhakṣayanti, teṣāṃ yadā tat paryavaity athāimam evākāśam abhiniṣpadyante\...*

\"They reach the Moon (*candra*). Having reached the Moon, they become food. There the gods consume them \[certain essence, the shell may return to earthly reincarnation\], just as King Soma waxes and wanes. **When their accumulated karma/merit is exhausted, they return along this same path back into space, from space to air, from air to rain, and from rain to Earth\...**\"

**(4). Pāḷi Canon Parallel: Rebirth in the Lunar Realm (*Candima-devaputta*)**

In the Pāḷi Suttas, the Moon is explicitly classified as a realm of worldly existence within the lower divine realms (*cātummahārājikā* / *tāvatiṃsa* sphere), where beings are bound by *kamma* and subject to death, decay, and involuntary rebirth.

**Samyutta Nikāya 2.9 (Candima Sutta):** *\"Namo te buddha vīratthu, vippamutto si sabbadhi; Sambādhappaṭipanno'smi, tassa me saraṇaṃ bhavā'ti.\"* \"Then the deity of the Moon (*Candima-devaputta*), seized by the Asura Rāhu, remembered the Blessed One and uttered this verse: *\'Homage to you, O Buddha, hero/vīra, you who are completely freed from all worldly existence! I have fallen into distress; be my refuge!\'*\"

**(5).** Sumerian Literatures

In ancient Mesopotamian and Sumerian cuneiform literature, the **Moon God**---known as **Nanna** in Sumerian and **Sîn** in Akkadian---is a **male deity**, the **primordial father of humanity**, and the **shepherd of souls and living beings**. Below are the key Sumerian tablets, hymns, and cuneiform texts that demonstrate Nanna/Sîn's role as the male paternal generator and protector of humankind.

**The Great Hymn to Nanna (Tablet CBS 13856 / Electronic Text Corpus of Sumerian Literature ETCSL 1.5.1)**

In this primary Sumerian liturgical text, Nanna is addressed directly as the **Father (*A-a*)**, the Creator of all living beings, and the supreme progenitor of humanity on Earth.

**Sumerian (Transliteration):** *a-a nanna en an-na an-dagal-la a-a nanna en di-ĝir-re-ne; a-a nanna lugal saĝ-ge₆-ga nam-lu₂-ulu₃ mu-un-dim₂-ma; u₃-mu-un saĝ-ge₆-ga ama-ar-gi₄ bi₂-in-gar*

\"O **Father Nanna** (Sumerian: *a-a nanna*), lord of heaven, vast in the sky! O **Father Nanna**, lord of the gods! O **Father Nanna**, king who created the black-headed people (humanity: *nam-lu₂-ulu₃*)! Lord of the black-headed ones, who established liberation for them!\"

This text explicitly identifies Nanna as **A-a** (Sumerian for \"Father\"). He is not merely a celestial body, but the active paternal creator (*mu-un-dim₂-ma*) of *nam-lu₂-ulu₃* (\"humanity\" or \"the black-headed people\").

**The Hymn \"Nanna-Suen's Cosmic Power\" (ETCSL 1.5.2)**

This Sumerian tablet highlights Nanna/Sîn as the **begetter** and the shepherd who collects, multiplies, and sustains all human souls and living creatures on Earth.

*a-a nanna za-e-me-en ab-zu-gin₇ maḫ-me-en; saĝ-ge₆-ga u₈-lu-a-bi2 za-a-ar mu-ra-an-du₈; za-e-me-en u₃-mu-un saĝ-du-ga mu-un-lu-lu-ne*

\"O **Father Nanna**, you are supreme like the deep abyss! The vast multitude of the black-headed people (humanity) is entrusted unto you. You are the lord who multiplies and propagates living beings!\"

The Sumerian verb *lu-lu* means \"to multiply / propagate abundantly.\" Nanna acts in the exact same paternal capacity as *Prajāpati* in the Vedic tradition---generating human life and holding stewardship over human souls.

**The Akkadian/Assyrian Hymn to Sîn (The Moon God as Universal Begetter)**

In the bilingual Akkadian/Sumerian tablets found in Nineveh and Nippur, the Moon God (Sîn) is invoked with titles that mirror the Vedic *Pitā* and *Prajāpati*.

**Akkadian Text Snippet:** *abi ilāni u amēlūti\... bānû kibrāt erbetti; šum-ma kīma ilu a-ba\... jâti-ma rēmu šuk-na-an-ni* \"O **Father of the gods and of humanity** (*abi ilāni u amēlūti*), creator of the four quarters of the earth! You who are like a compassionate **father** (*a-ba*), grant mercy unto me\...\"

**Nanna-Sîn in the Netherworld / Soul Receptacle Framework (ETCSL 1.5.3 - \"The Journey of Nanna to Nippur\")**

In Sumerian cosmology, the Moon God\'s journey across the sky and through the Underworld during the dark moon phase (*u₄-sakar*) positions him as a traveler between the realms of the living and the dead. During the end of the lunar month (the *Akkattu* / new moon phase), Nanna descends into the Netherworld (*Kur* / *Irkalla*) to decree destinies for departed spirits (*Gidim*) and pass judgment alongside Utu (the Sun God) before returning to the heavens to bestow new life upon Earth.

Salutation (namaḥ) from the front (purastāt), and then (atha) from behind (pṛṣṭhataḥ) unto You (te); let there be (astu) salutation (namo) unto You (te) from every side (sarvataḥ) indeed (eva), O All / Universal Being (sarva)! For You are (asi) the Father (pitā) of the world (lokasya) of the moving and non-moving (carācarasya); You (tvam) are of this \[world\] (asya) worthy of worship (pūjyaḥ) and (ca) the most venerable (garīyān) teacher (guruḥ). No (na) one equal to You (tvat-samaḥ) exists (asti); whence (kutaḥ) could there be another (anyaḥ) superior (abhyadhikaḥ) even (api) in the three worlds (loka-traye), O One of immeasurable power (apratima-prabhāva)!

**[44](javascript:void(0))   tasmāt praṇamya praṇidhāya kāyaṁ, prasādaye tvām aham īśam īḍyam; piteva putrasya sakheva sakhyuḥ, priyaḥ priyāyārhasi deva soḍhum.**

Therefore (tasmāt), bowing down (praṇamya) and prostrating (praṇidhāya) my body (kāyam), I (aham) implore the grace of (prasādaye) You (tvām), the praise-worthy (īḍyam) Lord (īśam); as a father (pitā) to his son (putrasya), as a friend (sakhā) to his friend (sakhyuḥ), as a lover (priyaḥ) to his beloved (priyāyāḥ), You ought (arhasi), O Deva (deva), to bear with / forgive \[my faults\] (soḍhum)!

***Sañjaya uvāca***

**[50](javascript:void(0))   ity arjunaṁ vāsudevas tathoktvā, svakaṁ rūpaṁ darśayām āsa bhūyaḥ; āśvāsayām āsa ca bhītam enaṁ, bhūtvā punaḥ saumya-vapur mahatma.**

Sañjaya said: Thus (iti) having spoken (uktvā) to Arjuna (arjunam) in that manner (tathā), Vāsudeva \[Krishna, the deva who abides throughout all creation while remaining unconditioned\] (vāsudevaḥ) showed (darśayām āsa) His own (svakam) form (rūpam) again (bhūyaḥ); and (ca) having become (bhūtvā) once more (punaḥ) of gentle form / benign body (saumya-vapuḥ), the Great Ātmā (mahā-ātmā) comforted / reassured (āśvāsayām āsa) him who was terrified (bhītam enam).

***Arjuna uvāca***

**[51](javascript:void(0))   dṛṣṭvedaṁ mānuṣaṁ rūpaṁ, tava saumyaṁ janārdana;**

**       idānīm asmi saṁvṛttaḥ, sa-cetāḥ prakṛtiṁ gataḥ.**

Arjuna said: Having seen (dṛṣṭvā) this (idam) gentle (saumyam) human (mānuṣam) form (rūpam) of Yours (tava), O Agitator of People (janārdana), now (idānīm) I have become (asmi saṁvṛttaḥ) composed in mind (sa-cetāḥ) and restored to my normal state (prakṛtiṁ gataḥ)!

***\***

***Śrī-Bhagavān uvāca***

**[52](javascript:void(0))   su-durdarśam idaṁ rūpaṁ, dṛṣṭavān asi yan mama;**

Bhagava Kṛṣṇa said: Very difficult to see (su-durdarśam) is this (idam) form (rūpam) of Mine (mama) which (yat) you have seen (dṛṣṭavān asi); even the devas (devāḥ api) are constantly (nityam) longing for seeing (darśana-kāṅkṣiṇaḥ) this (asya) form (rūpasya)!

**[53](javascript:void(0))   nāhaṁ vedair na tapasā, na dānena na cejyayā;**

**       śakya evaṁ-vidho draṣṭuṁ, dṛṣṭavān asi māṁ yathā.**

Not (na) by the Vedas (vedaiḥ), not (na) by primordial force (tapasā), not (na) by generosity/ charity (dānena), and (ca) not (na) by ritual worship (ijyayā) can I (aham) in such a manner (evaṁ-vidhaḥ) be seen (draṣṭum) as (yathā) you have seen (dṛṣṭavān asi) Me (mām)!

**[54](javascript:void(0))   bhaktyā tv ananyayā śakya, aham evaṁ-vidho 'rjuna;**

**       jñātuṁ draṣṭuṁ ca tattvena, praveṣṭuṁ ca parantapa.**

By exclusive devotion (bhaktyā tu ananyayā), however, can I (aham śakyaḥ) in such a manner (evaṁ-vidhaḥ), O Arjuna (arjuna), be known (jñātum), seen (draṣṭum), and (ca) entered (praveṣṭum) in truth / reality (tattvena), O Scorcher of Foes (parantapa)!

**[55](javascript:void(0))   mat-karma-kṛn mat-paramo, mad-bhaktaḥ saṇga-varjitaḥ;**

He who acts for Me (mat-karma-kṛt), who holds Me as the supreme goal (mat-paramaḥ), who is dedicated to Me (mad-bhaktaḥ), free from worldly attachment (saṅga-varjitaḥ), and without enmity (nirvairaḥ) toward all living beings (sarva-bhūteṣu)---he (saḥ) comes to Me (mām eti), O Son of Pāṇḍu (pāṇḍava)!
